Explanation: Using the quadratic formula: z = [-b ± √(b²-4ac)]/2a = [-4 ± √(4² - 415)]/2 = [-4 ± √(16-20)]/2 = [-4 ± √(-4)]/2 = [-4 ± 2i]/2 = -2 ± i.

Q266. Which quadrant contains the complex number z = -3 - 5i?
A) First
B) Second
C) Third
D) Fourth

Answer: C
Explanation: A complex number a+bi corresponds to the point (a,b). The point (-3,-5) is in the third quadrant (both coordinates negative).

Q267. Using Euler's formula, cos(x) can be expressed as:
A) (e^(ix) + e^(-ix)) / 2
B) (e^(ix) - e^(-ix)) / 2
C) (e^(ix) + e^(-ix)) / 2i
D) (e^(x) - e^(-x)) / 2

Answer: A
Explanation: e^(ix)=cos(x)+isin(x) and e^(-ix)=cos(x)-isin(x). Adding these two equations gives e^(ix)+e^(-ix)=2cos(x), so cos(x)=(e^(ix)+e^(-ix))/2.

Q268. The sum of the cube roots of 1 is:
A) 1
B) 3
C) i
D) 0

Answer: D
Explanation: The n-th roots of unity for n>1 always sum to 0.

Q269. The geometric arrangement of the n-th roots of unity in the Argand diagram is:
A) A straight line
B) The vertices of a regular n-sided polygon.
C) A circle with radius n.
D) A parabola.

Answer: B
Explanation: The n-th roots of unity are equally spaced on the unit circle (|z|=1) and form the vertices of a regular n-gon, with one vertex always at (1,0).

Q270. If z = a + bi, what is the geometric interpretation of the transformation z -> iz?
A) Reflection across the real axis.
B) Reflection across the imaginary axis.
C) Rotation by 90° counter-clockwise about the origin.
D) Rotation by 180° about the origin.

Answer: C
Explanation: The number i has modulus 1 and argument π/2 (90°). Multiplying a complex number by i multiplies the moduli (r*1=r) and adds the arguments (φ+π/2). This corresponds to a rotation by 90° counter-clockwise.

Q271. Simplify (1 + i)⁶.
A) -8
B) 8i
C) -8i
D) 8

Answer: C
Explanation: 1+i = √2(cos(π/4)+isin(π/4)). (1+i)⁶ = (√2)⁶[cos(6π/4)+isin(6π/4)] = 8[cos(3π/2)+isin(3π/2)] = 8(0 + i(-1)) = -8i.

Q272. Find a value for arg(z₁/z₂), if arg(z₁) = π/2 and arg(z₂) = π/3.
A) 5π/6
B) π/6
C) π²/6
D) -π/6

Answer: B
Explanation: The rule for division is arg(z₁/z₂) = arg(z₁) - arg(z₂) = π/2 - π/3 = 3π/6 - 2π/6 = π/6.

Chapter 2: Binomial Theorem and Series Expansions (Continued)

Q273. The expression nCr is equivalent to:
A) nPr
B) nC(n-r)
C) n!
D) r!

Answer: B
Explanation: This is a fundamental property of binomial coefficients. Choosing r items to take is the same as choosing n-r items to leave behind. So, nCr = nC(n-r).

Q274. What is the coefficient of x³y² in the expansion of (x + y)⁵?
A) 1
B) 5
C) 10
D) 20

Answer: C
Explanation: The term with x³y² has coefficient ⁵C₂ (or ⁵C₃, they are equal). ⁵C₂ = 10.

Q275. Find the term independent of x in the expansion of (x - 1/x²)⁶.
A) -20
B) 20
C) 15
D) -15

Answer: C
Explanation: The general term is T_(r+1) = ⁶Cᵣ(x)⁶⁻ʳ(-1/x²)ʳ = ⁶Cᵣ(-1)ʳ x⁶⁻ʳ x⁻²ʳ = ⁶Cᵣ(-1)ʳ x⁶⁻³ʳ. For the term to be independent of x, the power of x must be 0. 6-3r=0 => r=2. The term is ⁶C₂(-1)² = 15(1) = 15.

Q276. What is the sum of the coefficients in the expansion of (x + y)⁶?
A) 0
B) 1
C) 32
D) 64

Answer: D
Explanation: To find the sum of coefficients, let x=1 and y=1. The sum is (1+1)⁶ = 2⁶ = 64.

Q277. The first two terms of the expansion of (1 - 3x)ⁿ are 1 - 12x. Find the value of n.
A) 3
B) 4
C) 6
D) -4

Answer: B
Explanation: The expansion is (1-3x)ⁿ = 1 + n(-3x) + ... = 1 - 3nx + ... We are given this is 1 - 12x. Equating the x terms: -3nx = -12x => -3n = -12 => n=4.

Q278. In the expansion of (1+ax)⁸, the coefficient of x² is 252. Find the value of a.
A) ±3
B) ±9
C) ±1
D) ±1/3

Answer: A
Explanation: The x² term is ⁸C₂(ax)² = 28a²x². The coefficient is 28a². We are given 28a² = 252. a² = 252/28 = 9. So a = ±3.

Q279. The Maclaurin series for sin(x) is x - x³/3! + x⁵/5! - ... For this series to be valid, x must be in:
A) degrees
B) radians
C) |x|<1
D) x>0

Answer: B
Explanation: The standard calculus-derived series for trigonometric functions like sin(x) and cos(x) are only valid when the angle x is measured in radians.

Q280. Find the first three terms of the expansion of √(1 - 2x).
A) 1 - x - x²/2
B) 1 + x + x²/2
C) 1 - x + x²/4
D) 1 + x - x²/4

Answer: A
Explanation: This is (1-2x)¹/². Use (1+y)ⁿ ≈ 1 + ny + n(n-1)/2! y² with y=-2x, n=1/2.
1 + (1/2)(-2x) + (1/2)(-1/2)/2² = 1 - x - (1/8)(4x²) = 1 - x - x²/2.

Q281. For what range of x is the expansion of (1-4x)⁻³ valid?
A) |x| < 3
B) |x| < 4
C) |x| < 1/3
D) |x| < 1/4

Answer: D
Explanation: The expansion of (1+y)ⁿ is valid for |y|<1. Here y = -4x. So |-4x|<1 => 4|x|<1 => |x|<1/4.

Q282. Find an approximation for ³√1.06 using the binomial theorem.
A) 1.02
B) 1.03
C) 1.01
D) 1.06

Answer: A
Explanation: (1+0.06)¹/³. Use (1+x)ⁿ ≈ 1+nx.
≈ 1 + (1/3)(0.06) = 1 + 0.02 = 1.02.

Q283. What row of Pascal's triangle gives the coefficients for the expansion of (a+b)⁶?
A) Row 5
B) Row 6
C) Row 7
D) Row 8

Answer: B
Explanation: Row n of Pascal's triangle gives the coefficients for the power n. We need Row 6 (starting from Row 0). The values are 1 6 15 20 15 6 1.

Q284. Which term represents the general term in the expansion of (a+x)ⁿ?
A) T_r = nCr aⁿ⁻ʳ xʳ
B) T_(r+1) = nCr aⁿ⁻ʳ xʳ
C) T_r = nPr aⁿ⁻ʳ xʳ
D) T_(n+1) = nCn a⁰xⁿ

Answer: B
Explanation: By convention, the first term corresponds to r=0. So the (r+1)-th term is given by T_(r+1) = nCr aⁿ⁻ʳ xʳ.

Q285. In the Maclaurin series for cos(x), which powers of x are present?
A) All powers
B) Only odd powers
C) Only even powers
D) No powers, only constants

Answer: C
Explanation: The series for cos(x) is 1 - x²/2! + x⁴/4! - ... It contains only the constant term and even powers of x because cos(x) is an even function.

Q286. Use the binomial expansion of (1+x)ⁿ to find the expansion of 1/(1-x).
A) 1 - x + x² - x³ + ...
B) 1 + x + x² + x³ + ...
C) 1 - 2x + 3x² - ...
D) 1 + 2x + 3x² + ...

Answer: B
Explanation: 1/(1-x) = (1-x)⁻¹. Use (1+y)ⁿ with n=-1, y=-x.
1 + (-1)(-x) + (-1)(-2)/2!² + ... = 1 + x + x² + ...

Q287. Which value cannot be approximated using the expansion of (1+x)¹/²?
A) √1.5
B) √0.9
C) √2
D) √1.1

Answer: C
Explanation: The expansion (1+x)¹/² is valid for |x|<1. To approximate √2, you would need to write it as √(1+1), where x=1. This is not in the interval of convergence (-1, 1).

Chapter 3: Polynomials and Rational Functions (Continued)

Q288. When dividing the polynomial 2x³+3x-1 by x+2 using synthetic division, the number used for the division is:
A) 2
B) -2
C) 1
D) -1

Answer: B
Explanation: Synthetic division divides by a linear binomial (x - c). Here, the binomial is x+2, which is x - (-2). So c = -2.

Q289. Find the quotient Q(x) when x³ - 5x² + 7x - 2 is divided by (x - 2).
A) x² - 3x + 1
B) x² + 3x - 1
C) x² - 7x + 21
D) x + 1

Answer: A
Explanation: Using synthetic division with c=2:
2 | 1 -5 7 -2
| 2 -6 2
1  -3   1   0


The quotient is the bottom line (excluding the remainder): 1x² - 3x + 1.

Q290. If P(c)=0, P'(c)=0, and P''(c)≠0 for a polynomial P(x), then at x=c, the graph has a...
A) simple root.
B) root of even multiplicity (touches).
C) root of odd multiplicity > 1 (inflection).
D) vertical asymptote.

Answer: B
Explanation: P(c)=0 means it's a root. P'(c)=0 means the tangent is horizontal. P''(c)≠0 means it's a local min or max, so the graph is tangent to the x-axis and turns around, which is characteristic of a root of even multiplicity (like a double root).

Q291. Which of the following is a possible rational root of the polynomial 3x³ - 5x² + x + 6 = 0, according to the Rational Root Theorem?
A) 5/3
B) 4
C) 2/3
D) 3/2

Answer: C
Explanation: Rational roots must be of the form p/q, where p is a factor of the constant term (6) and q is a factor of the leading coefficient (3). Factors of 6 are ±1,±2,±3,±6. Factors of 3 are ±1,±3. Possible rational roots include ±1, ±2, ±3, ±6, ±1/3, ±2/3. Of the options, only 2/3 fits this pattern.

Q292. The y-intercept of the polynomial P(x) = a_n x^n + ... + a_1 x + a_0 is:
A) a_n
B) a_1
C) a_0
D) 0

Answer: C
Explanation: The y-intercept occurs when x=0. All terms with x become zero, leaving only the constant term a₀. P(0) = a₀.

Q293. The end behavior of y = 5x³ - 2x² + 1 is:
A) Falls left, Rises right
B) Rises left, Falls right
C) Rises left, Rises right
D) Falls left, Falls right

Answer: A
Explanation: Leading term is 5x³. The degree (3) is odd and the leading coefficient (5) is positive. Odd degree means ends go in opposite directions. Positive coefficient means it rises to the right. So, it falls left and rises right.

Q294. A rational function f(x) = P(x)/Q(x) has a hole in the graph at x=c if...
A) Q(c)=0 and P(c)≠0
B) P(c)=0 and Q(c)≠0
C) P(c)=0 and Q(c)=0
D) The degree of P is greater than the degree of Q.

Answer: C
Explanation: If both numerator and denominator are zero at x=c, it means there's a common factor of (x-c) that can be cancelled out, leading to a removable discontinuity (a hole) instead of a vertical asymptote.

Q295. Find the slant asymptote for f(x) = (2x² - 3x + 1) / (x - 1).
A) y = 2x - 3
B) y = 2x + 1
C) y = 2x
D) y = 2x - 1

Answer: D
Explanation: Since degree of numerator is one more than denominator, perform polynomial long division.
2x - 1
x-1|2x² - 3x + 1
-(2x² - 2x)
-x + 1
   -(-x + 1)
   ----------
          0
IGNORE_WHEN_COPYING_START
content_copy
download
Use code with caution.
IGNORE_WHEN_COPYING_END

The quotient is 2x - 1. So the slant asymptote is y = 2x - 1.

Q296. The graph of a rational function can cross which type of asymptote?
A) Vertical
B) Horizontal
C) It cannot cross any asymptote.
D) Both vertical and horizontal.

Answer: B
Explanation: A graph can never cross a vertical asymptote, as the function is undefined there. However, it can cross a horizontal or slant asymptote, as these only describe the end behavior (as x -> ±∞).

Q297. Find the roots of the polynomial equation (x-2)²(x+3) = 0.
A) 2, -3
B) -2, 3
C) 4, -3
D) 2 (multiplicity 2), -3

Answer: D
Explanation: The factors are (x-2) and (x+3). Setting them to zero gives roots x=2 and x=-3. Since the factor (x-2) is squared, the root x=2 has a multiplicity of 2.

Q298. How does the graph of y=(x+1)³ behave at its x-intercept?
A) It crosses the axis like a straight line.
B) It touches the axis and turns around.
C) It crosses the axis but flattens out (an inflection point on the axis).
D) It has a vertical asymptote.

Answer: C
Explanation: A root of odd multiplicity greater than 1 (like 3) causes the graph to cross the x-axis while being momentarily horizontal, creating a point of inflection on the axis.

Q299. To find the remainder of P(x)=x¹⁰⁰ - 2x + 5 divided by (x+1), you should calculate:
A) P(1)
B) P(-1)
C) P(0)
D) The full division

Answer: B
Explanation: The Remainder Theorem is the most efficient method. To find the remainder when dividing by (x - c), you calculate P(c). Here c=-1. So we calculate P(-1) = (-1)¹⁰⁰ - 2(-1) + 5 = 1 + 2 + 5 = 8.

Chapter 4: Logarithmic and Exponential Functions (Continued)

Q300. The domain of the function f(x) = log(x-2) is:
A) x > 0
B) x < 2
C) x > 2
D) All real numbers

Answer: C
Explanation: The argument of a logarithm must be positive. Therefore, x-2 > 0, which means x > 2.

Q301. Evaluate log₂(1/8).
A) 3
B) -3
C) 1/3
D) 4

Answer: B
Explanation: 1/8 = 1/2³ = 2⁻³. So, we are asking "2 to what power equals 2⁻³?". The answer is -3.

Q302. Solve for x: 5^(2x-1) = 25.
A) x = 3/2
B) x = 2
C) x = 1
D) x = 1/2

Answer: A
Explanation: Write both sides with the same base: 5^(2x-1) = 5². Equate the exponents: 2x-1 = 2 => 2x = 3 => x = 3/2.

Q303. Express 3 ln(a) - (1/2)ln(b) as a single logarithm.
A) ln(3a / √b)
B) ln(a³√b)
C) ln(a³ / √b)
D) ln(a³ - b/2 )

Answer: C
Explanation: 3ln(a) = ln(a³) and (1/2)ln(b) = ln(b¹/²) = ln(√b). Then ln(a³) - ln(√b) = ln(a³/√b).

Q304. Solve for x: ln(x) = -2.
A) x = e²
B) x = 1/e
C) x = e⁻²
D) x = -2e

Answer: C
Explanation: Rewrite the logarithmic equation ln(x)=-2 (which is log_e(x)=-2) in exponential form: x = e⁻².

Q305. The graph of y = (1/3)^x represents:
A) Exponential growth
B) Exponential decay
C) A linear function
D) A logarithmic function

Answer: B
Explanation: In y=b^x, if 0 < b < 1, the function represents exponential decay. Here b = 1/3.

Q306. A substance has a half-life of 20 days. What fraction of the original substance remains after 60 days?
A) 1/3
B) 1/4
C) 1/6
D) 1/8

Answer: D
Explanation: 60 days is equal to 60/20 = 3 half-lives. After 1 half-life, 1/2 remains. After 2, (1/2)²=1/4 remains. After 3, (1/2)³=1/8 remains.

Q307. How long will it take for an investment to double at a rate of 10% compounded continuously? (approx)
A) 7 years
B) 10 years
C) 5 years
D) 2 years

Answer: A
Explanation: Use A = Pe^(rt). We want A=2P. So 2P = Pe^(0.1t) => 2 = e^(0.1t). ln(2) = 0.1t => t = ln(2)/0.1. Since ln(2) ≈ 0.693, t ≈ 0.693/0.1 = 6.93 years. The "Rule of 70" (or 69.3) gives a quick estimate: 70/10 = 7 years.

Q308. Solve the equation 4^x - 2^x - 2 = 0.
A) x = 1
B) x = 2
C) x = 0
D) x = -1

Answer: A
Explanation: Let y = 2^x. Then 4^x = (2²)ˣ = (2ˣ)² = y². The equation becomes y² - y - 2 = 0.
Factoring: (y-2)(y+1) = 0. So y=2 or y=-1.
Substitute back: 2^x = 2 => x=1. 2^x = -1 has no real solution. So x=1 is the only solution.

Q309. The pH of a solution is given by pH = -log[H⁺]. If the pH is 4, what is the concentration [H⁺]?
A) 10⁻⁴
B) 10⁴
C) 4
D) -4

Answer: A
Explanation: 4 = -log[H⁺] => -4 = log₁₀[H⁺]. Rewrite in exponential form: [H⁺] = 10⁻⁴.

Q310. The graph of f(x) = a^x (for a>1) and g(x) = log_a(x) are reflections of each other across the line...
A) y = 0
B) x = 0
C) y = x
D) y = -x

Answer: C
Explanation: Exponential and logarithmic functions with the same base are inverses of each other. The graphs of inverse functions are always reflections across the line y=x.

Chapter 5: Limits, Continuity, and Differentiation (Continued)

Q311. Evaluate lim (x→0) [sin(x) / x].
A) 0
B) 1
C) ∞
D) Does not exist

Answer: B
Explanation: This is a fundamental trigonometric limit. Although direct substitution gives 0/0, it is a standard result that lim (x→0) [sin(x) / x] = 1.

Q312. Find the derivative of y = 5x⁴ - 2x² + 7.
A) 20x³ - 4x + 7
B) 20x³ - 4x
C) 4x³ - 2x
D) 20x⁵ - 4x³ + 7x

Answer: B
Explanation: Apply the power rule to each term. The derivative of a constant (7) is 0.
d/dx(5x⁴) - d/dx(2x²) + d/dx(7) = 20x³ - 4x + 0.

Q313. Find the derivative of y = sec(x).
A) tan(x)
B) sec²(x)
C) sec(x)tan(x)
D) -csc(x)cot(x)

Answer: C
Explanation: This is a standard derivative rule for trigonometric functions.

Q314. Using the chain rule, find the derivative of y = e^(sin x).
A) cos(x) e^(sin x)
B) sin(x) e^(sin x)
C) e^(cos x)
D) e^(sin x)

Answer: A
Explanation: Let u = sin(x), so y = e^u. dy/dx = (dy/du)(du/dx) = (e^u)(cos x) = e^(sin x) cos(x).

Q315. Find the second derivative (f''(x)) of f(x) = x³ + 2x² - x.
A) 3x² + 4x - 1
B) 6x + 4
C) 6x
D) 6

Answer: B
Explanation: First derivative: f'(x) = 3x² + 4x - 1.
Second derivative: f''(x) = d/dx(3x² + 4x - 1) = 6x + 4.

Q316. The points where the second derivative f''(x) is zero or undefined and the concavity changes are called:
A) Critical points
B) Local minima
C) Local maxima
D) Points of inflection

Answer: D
Explanation: A point of inflection is a point on a curve at which the sign of the curvature (i.e., the concavity) changes. This occurs where f''(x) changes sign.

Q317. If a function f(x) is decreasing, what must be true about its derivative f'(x)?
A) f'(x) > 0
B) f'(x) < 0
C) f'(x) = 0
D) f'(x) is constant

Answer: B
Explanation: A negative first derivative indicates that the function's slope is negative, meaning it is decreasing.

Q318. Find the equation of the normal line to the curve y = x³ at x = 1.
A) y - 1 = 3(x-1)
B) y - 1 = (-1/3)(x-1)
C) y = 3x - 2
D) y = -3x + 4

Answer: B
Explanation: First find the slope of the tangent. y' = 3x². At x=1, m_tan = 3(1)² = 3.
The slope of the normal is the negative reciprocal: m_norm = -1/3.
The point on the curve is (1, 1³), i.e., (1,1).
The equation of the normal line is y - y₁ = m(x-x₁), which is y - 1 = (-1/3)(x-1).

Q319. The process of finding the derivative of an equation that implicitly defines y as a function of x is called:
A) The chain rule
B) Implicit differentiation
C) Logarithmic differentiation
D) Partial differentiation

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of implicit differentiation, used when you cannot easily solve the equation for y.

Q320. If x² + y² = 25, find dy/dx.
A) x/y
B) -x/y
C) y/x
D) 2x+2y

Answer: B
Explanation: Differentiate both sides with respect to x: 2x + 2y(dy/dx) = 0.
Solve for dy/dx: 2y(dy/dx) = -2x => dy/dx = -2x / 2y = -x/y.

Q321. The value c that satisfies the Mean Value Theorem for derivatives for f(x) on [a,b] is where:
A) The instantaneous rate of change equals the average rate of change.
B) The function value equals the average function value.
C) The instantaneous rate of change is zero.
D) The function value is zero.

Answer: A
Explanation: The MVT states that f'(c) = [f(b)-f(a)]/[b-a]. f'(c) is the instantaneous rate of change, and the right side is the average rate of change over the interval.

Q322. To find the intervals where a function is concave up or down, one must analyze the sign of the...
A) Function itself, f(x)
B) First derivative, f'(x)
C) Second derivative, f''(x)
D) Third derivative, f'''(x)

Answer: C
Explanation: The sign of the second derivative determines concavity. f''>0 means concave up, f''<0 means concave down.

Q323. A function has a critical point at x=c if...
A) f(c) = 0
B) f'(c) = 0 or f'(c) is undefined.
C) f''(c) = 0
D) The function is continuous at c.

Answer: B
Explanation: Critical points are potential locations for local maxima or minima, and they occur where the tangent line is horizontal (f'=0) or vertical/non-existent (f' is undefined).

Chapter 6: Integration (Continued)

Q324. What does the "+ C" represent in an indefinite integral?
A) A specific constant.
B) A correction factor.
C) The constant of integration.
D) A critical value.

Answer: C
Explanation: Since the derivative of any constant is zero, the antiderivative of a function is not unique but represents a family of functions that differ by a constant. This is the constant of integration.

Q325. Evaluate ∫ 5 dx.
A) 5
B) 0
C) 5x
D) 5x + C

Answer: D
Explanation: The integral of a constant k is kx + C. So, ∫ 5 dx = 5x + C.

Q326. Evaluate ∫ (3x² - 2x + 1) dx.
A) 6x - 2 + C
B) x³ - x² + x + C
C) x³/3 - x²/2 + x + C
D) 3x³ - 2x² + x + C

Answer: B
Explanation: Integrate term by term using the power rule: 3(x³/3) - 2(x²/2) + 1(x) + C = x³ - x² + x + C.

Q327. Find the area under the curve y = 1/x from x = 1 to x = e.
A) 1
B) e
C) 0
D) e - 1

Answer: A
Explanation: Area = ∫[1,e] (1/x) dx = [ln|x|]_[1,e] = ln(e) - ln(1) = 1 - 0 = 1.

Q328. Use u-substitution to evaluate ∫ sin(x)cos(x) dx.
A) (1/2)sin²(x) + C
B) (1/2)cos²(x) + C
C) -sin(x)cos(x) + C
D) sin(x) - cos(x) + C

Answer: A
Explanation: Let u = sin(x). Then du = cos(x)dx. The integral becomes ∫ u du = u²/2 + C = (1/2)sin²(x) + C. (Letting u=cos(x) is also possible and gives -(1/2)cos²(x) + C, which differs only by a constant).

Q329. If ∫[0,5] f(x) dx = 10 and ∫[0,5] g(x) dx = 3, what is ∫[0,5] [2f(x) - g(x)] dx?
A) 7
B) 17
C) 23
D) 14

Answer: B
Explanation: Using properties of integrals: 2∫f(x)dx - ∫g(x)dx = 2(10) - 3 = 20 - 3 = 17.

Q330. For integration by parts (∫udv = uv - ∫vdu), if evaluating ∫x e²ˣ dx, what is a good choice for dv?
A) x
B) dx
C) e²ˣ dx
D) e²ˣ

Answer: C
Explanation: Using LIATE, u = x (algebraic) and the rest is dv. So dv = e²ˣ dx. This is a good choice because dv is easily integrated (v = ½e²ˣ).

Q331. The first step in integrating a rational function P(x)/Q(x) where degree(P) ≥ degree(Q) is to:
A) Use partial fractions.
B) Use integration by parts.
C) Factor the denominator.
D) Perform polynomial long division.

Answer: D
Explanation: You must first divide the numerator by the denominator to get a polynomial plus a proper rational function (where numerator degree < denominator degree) before applying other techniques like partial fractions.

Q332. The partial fraction decomposition for (3x+1) / (x-1)(x+2) is of the form:
A) A/x + B/(x-1) + C/(x+2)
B) A/(x-1) + B/(x+2)
C) Ax+B / (x-1)(x+2)
D) A/(x-1)² + B/(x+2)

Answer: B
Explanation: Since the denominator has two distinct linear factors, the decomposition will have two terms, each with a constant numerator and one of the factors as the denominator.

Q333. Find the volume of the solid formed by rotating the line y=x from x=0 to x=2 about the x-axis.
A) 8π/3
B) 4π
C) 2π
D) 8π

Answer: A
Explanation: Using the Disk Method: Volume = ∫[0,2] π y² dx = ∫[0,2] π x² dx.
V = π [x³/3]_[0,2] = π (2³/3 - 0³/3) = 8π/3.

Q334. If you use substitution u=2x in the definite integral ∫[1,3] f(2x) dx, the new limits of integration for u will be:
A) [1, 3]
B) [1/2, 3/2]
C) [2, 6]
D) [f(2), f(6)]

Answer: C
Explanation: You must change the limits to be in terms of u.
Lower limit: x=1 => u=2(1)=2.
Upper limit: x=3 => u=2(3)=6.
The new limits are [2, 6].

Q335. ∫ sec²(5x) dx = ?
A) (1/5)tan(5x) + C
B) tan(5x) + C
C) 5tan(5x) + C
D) (1/5)sec(5x)tan(5x) + C

Answer: A
Explanation: Use u-sub with u=5x, du=5dx. ∫sec²(u) (du/5) = (1/5)∫sec²(u)du = (1/5)tan(u)+C = (1/5)tan(5x)+C.

Chapter 7: Ordinary Differential Equations (Continued)

Q336. A differential equation is considered "linear" if:
A) It contains only first-order derivatives.
B) The dependent variable (y) and its derivatives appear to the first power.
C) The equation can be separated.
D) It can be graphed as a straight line.

Answer: B
Explanation: Linearity means that the unknown function y and its derivatives y', y'', etc., are not multiplied together, squared, or inside another function like sin(y) or e^y.

Q337. Solve the separable differential equation dy/dx = 2x/y.
A) y = x² + C
B) y² = 2x² + C
C) ln|y| = x² + C
D) y² = x + C

Answer: B
Explanation: Separate: y dy = 2x dx. Integrate: ∫ y dy = ∫ 2x dx => y²/2 = 2(x²/2) + C₁ => y²/2 = x² + C₁ => y² = 2x² + C (where C=2C₁).

Q338. Find a particular solution to dy/dx = 3, given the initial condition y(1) = 5.
A) y = 3x + 2
B) y = 3x + 5
C) y = 3x
D) y = 5

Answer: A
Explanation: General solution: y = ∫ 3 dx = 3x + C. Apply initial condition: 5 = 3(1) + C => 5 = 3 + C => C=2. Particular solution is y = 3x + 2.

Q339. Find the general solution for y'' + y' - 6y = 0.
A) y = C₁e^(3x) + C₂e^(-2x)
B) y = C₁e^(-3x) + C₂e^(2x)
C) y = (C₁+C₂x)e^(-3x)
D) y = C₁e^(6x) + C₂e^(x)

Answer: B
Explanation: Characteristic equation: m² + m - 6 = 0. Factoring: (m+3)(m-2) = 0. Roots are m₁=-3 and m₂=2. These are distinct real roots. General solution: y = C₁e^(-3x) + C₂e^(2x).

Q340. The motion described by m(d²x/dt²) + b(dx/dt) + kx = 0 is known as:
A) Simple Harmonic Motion
B) Damped Harmonic Motion
C) Forced Oscillation
D) Newton's Law of Cooling

Answer: B
Explanation: The term b(dx/dt) represents a damping force (proportional to velocity). Its presence turns Simple Harmonic Motion (SHM) into Damped Harmonic Motion.

Q341. For the DE y'' - 4y' + 13y = 0, the characteristic equation has roots m = 2 ± 3i. What is the general solution?
A) y = C₁e^(2x) + C₂e^(3x)
B) y = (C₁cos(3x)+C₂sin(3x))
C) y = e^(2x)(C₁cos(3x)+C₂sin(3x))
D) y = e^(3x)(C₁cos(2x)+C₂sin(2x))

Answer: C
Explanation: The roots are complex conjugates α ± βi with α=2 and β=3. The general solution is y = e^(αx)[C₁cos(βx)+C₂sin(βx)].

Q342. A general solution to a second-order ODE contains how many arbitrary constants?
A) 0
B) 1
C) 2
D) 3

Answer: C
Explanation: Each integration step introduces an arbitrary constant. Since a second-order DE requires two integrations to solve, its general solution will have two arbitrary constants (C₁ and C₂).

Q343. What kind of equation is ∂u/∂t = α (∂²u/∂x²)?
A) An Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE)
B) A Partial Differential Equation (PDE)
C) A characteristic equation
D) A linear first-order equation

Answer: B
Explanation: The equation contains partial derivatives (∂), which means the unknown function u depends on more than one independent variable (t and x). This is the definition of a PDE. It is the Heat Equation.

Chapter 8: Vectors in Two and Three Dimensions (Continued)

Q344. A unit vector has a magnitude of...
A) 0
B) 1
C) -1
D) π

Answer: B
Explanation: By definition, a unit vector is a vector with a magnitude (or length) of exactly 1.

Q345. Find a unit vector in the direction of v = <3, -4>.
A) <3/5, -4/5>
B) <3/7, -4/7>
C) <1, -1>
D) <3, -4>

Answer: A
Explanation: First find the magnitude: |v| = √(3²+(-4)²) = √25 = 5. To get a unit vector, divide the original vector by its magnitude: v/|v| = <3/5, -4/5>.

Q346. What is the projection of vector u = <2, 3> onto vector v = <4, 0>?
A) <2, 0>
B) <2, 3>
C) <4, 0>
D) 8

Answer: A
Explanation: Vector projection proj_v(u) = [(u·v)/|v|²]v.
u·v = 24 + 30 = 8. |v|² = 4² + 0² = 16.
proj_v(u) = (8/16)<4,0> = (1/2)<4,0> = <2,0>.

Q347. The work done by a constant force F⃗ in moving an object along a displacement vector d⃗ is calculated as:
A) F⃗ × d⃗
B) |F⃗||d⃗|
C) |F⃗ × d⃗|
D) F⃗ · d⃗

Answer: D
Explanation: Work is a scalar quantity defined by the dot product of the force and displacement vectors, W = F⃗ · d⃗.

Q348. What is the result of k · (i × j)? (Scalar Triple Product)
A) 0
B) 1
C) -1
D) j

Answer: B
Explanation: First, i × j = k. Then k · (i × j) = k · k. The dot product of a vector with itself is its magnitude squared. |k|² = 1² = 1.

Q349. The value of the scalar triple product u · (v × w) gives the...
A) Area of the parallelogram formed by u and v.
B) Volume of the parallelepiped formed by u, v, and w.
C) Area of the triangle formed by v and w.
D) Angle between u and v×w.

Answer: B
Explanation: The absolute value of the scalar triple product represents the volume of the parallelepiped with the three vectors as adjacent edges.

Q350. Find the symmetric equations for the line passing through (1, 0, 2) with direction vector <2, 3, 4>.
A) (x-1)/2 = y/3 = (z-2)/4
B) x/1 = y/0 = z/2
C) 2(x-1) = 3y = 4(z-2)
D) x=1+2t, y=3t, z=2+4t

Answer: A
Explanation: The symmetric form is (x-x₀)/a = (y-y₀)/b = (z-z₀)/c. Substituting the values gives (x-1)/2 = (y-0)/3 = (z-2)/4.

Q351. Find a normal vector for the plane defined by the points P(1,0,0), Q(0,1,0), and R(0,0,1).
A) <1,1,1>
B) <1,0,0>
C) <1,-1,0>
D) <-1,-1,1>

Answer: A
Explanation: Find two vectors in the plane, e.g., PQ = <-1,1,0> and PR = <-1,0,1>. The normal vector is their cross product: PQ×PR = |i j k| |-1 1 0| |-1 0 1| = i(1)-j(-1)+k(1) = <1,1,1>.

Q352. The planes 2x-y+3z=1 and 4x-2y+6z=5 are...
A) Perpendicular
B) The same plane
C) Intersecting but not perpendicular
D) Parallel

Answer: D
Explanation: Their normal vectors are n₁=<2,-1,3> and n₂=<4,-2,6>. Notice that n₂ = 2n₁. Since the normal vectors are parallel (scalar multiples of each other), the planes are parallel. They are not the same plane because the constant terms are not in the same ratio.

Q353. The angle between the planes x+y=1 and y+z=1 is:
A) 30°
B) 45°
C) 60°
D) 90°

Answer: C
Explanation: The angle between planes is the angle between their normal vectors. n₁=<1,1,0>, n₂=<0,1,1>. cosθ=(n₁·n₂)/(|n₁||n₂|) = (10+11+0*1)/(√(2)√(2)) = 1/2. θ = arccos(1/2) = 60°.

Q354. Which field associates a scalar value with every point in space?
A) Vector Field
B) Scalar Field
C) Force Field
D) Magnetic Field

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of a scalar field, e.g., temperature or pressure in a room.

Q355. Which physical quantity is best represented by a vector field?
A) Temperature
B) Pressure
C) Wind Velocity
D) Density

Answer: C
Explanation: Wind has both a magnitude (speed) and a direction at every point, making it a vector quantity that forms a vector field.

Q356. Find the distance from the point (1,1,5) to the plane x+2y+2z=4.
A) 3
B) 9
C) 1
D) 5

Answer: A
Explanation: The distance from (x₁,y₁,z₁) to Ax+By+Cz+D=0 is |Ax₁+By₁+Cz₁+D|/√(A²+B²+C²).
The plane is x+2y+2z-4=0. Point is (1,1,5). A=1, B=2, C=2, D=-4.
Distance = |1(1)+2(1)+2(5)-4|/√(1²+2²+2²) = |1+2+10-4|/√9 = |9|/3 = 3.

Chapter 9: Matrices and Determinants (Continued)

Q357. The main diagonal of a square matrix consists of elements a_ij where...
A) i > j
B) i < j
C) i = j
D) i + j = n+1

Answer: C
Explanation: The main (or principal) diagonal runs from the top left to the bottom right and consists of elements where the row index equals the column index (a₁₁, a₂₂, a₃₃, ...).

Q358. If A is a 3x2 matrix and B is a 2x4 matrix, what is the order of matrix AB?
A) 3x4
B) 2x2
C) 4x3
D) Not defined

Answer: A
Explanation: The inner dimensions (2 and 2) match, so the product is defined. The resulting matrix has the outer dimensions: 3 rows and 4 columns (3x4).

Q359. A matrix that is equal to its own transpose (A = A^T) is called:
A) Skew-symmetric
B) Symmetric
C) An identity matrix
D) A diagonal matrix

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of a symmetric matrix, where a_ij = a_ji.

Q360. If A and B are invertible matrices, then (AB)⁻¹ = ?
A) A⁻¹B⁻¹
B) B⁻¹A⁻¹
C) (BA)⁻¹
D) A B

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the "socks and shoes" rule for matrix inverses (and transposes). The order of the product is reversed when taking the inverse.

Q361. Find the determinant of A = [[1, 0, 5], [2, 1, 6], [3, 4, 0]].
A) 1
B) -1
C) 21
D) -19

Answer: A
Explanation: Expand along the first row: 1det([[1,6],[4,0]]) - 0... + 5det([[2,1],[3,4]]) = 1(10 - 64) + 5(24 - 1*3) = 1(-24) + 5(8-3) = -24 + 5(5) = -24 + 25 = 1.

Q362. What does Cramer's Rule calculate directly?
A) The inverse of a matrix.
B) The determinant of a matrix.
C) The solution to a system of linear equations.
D) The rank of a matrix.

Answer: C
Explanation: Cramer's rule is a method that uses determinants to find the unique solution (x, y, z, etc.) of a system of linear equations AX=B, provided det(A)≠0.

Q363. The matrix system AX=B represents a system of linear equations. B is the...
A) Coefficient matrix
B) Variable matrix
C) Solution matrix
D) Constant matrix

Answer: D
Explanation: In AX=B, A is the matrix of coefficients, X is the column matrix of variables, and B is the column matrix of constants on the right-hand side of the equations.

Q364. An elementary row operation (ERO) for solving systems with augmented matrices is:
A) Deleting a row.
B) Adding a constant to a row.
C) Multiplying a row by a non-zero constant.
D) Squaring a row.

Answer: C
Explanation: The three EROs are: 1) swapping two rows, 2) multiplying a row by a non-zero constant, and 3) adding a multiple of one row to another row.

Q365. The goal of Gauss-Jordan elimination is to transform the augmented matrix [A|B] into the form...
A) [I | X], where I is the identity matrix and X is the solution.
B) [A⁻¹ | B]
C) A row-echelon form requiring back-substitution.
D) [A^T | B]

Answer: A
Explanation: Gauss-Jordan elimination continues past the row-echelon form to create a reduced row-echelon form, which ideally is the identity matrix on the left side, allowing the solution vector to be read directly from the right side.

Q366. A system of equations with a determinant of its coefficient matrix equal to zero might have...
A) A unique solution.
B) No solution or infinitely many solutions.
C) Only the trivial solution.
D) No solutions, always.

Answer: B
Explanation: A zero determinant means the matrix is singular and not invertible. This corresponds to a dependent or inconsistent system, which will either have no solution (e.g., parallel lines) or infinitely many solutions (e.g., coincident lines).

Q367. The cofactor C_ij of a matrix is defined as:
A) The minor M_ij
B) (-1)^ij M_ij
C) (-1)^(i+j) M_ij
D) (i+j) M_ij

Answer: C
Explanation: The cofactor is the signed minor. The sign is determined by (-1)^(i+j), which creates the "checkerboard" pattern of signs.

Chapter 10: Introduction to Vector Spaces (Continued)

Q368. The set of all polynomials of degree exactly 3 is NOT a vector space because:
A) It does not contain the zero vector.
B) It is not closed under addition.
C) It is not closed under scalar multiplication.
D) It is a vector space.

Answer: B
Explanation: The sum of two polynomials of degree exactly 3 can result in a polynomial of a lower degree. For example, (x³ + 2x) + (-x³ + 5x) = 7x, which is not degree 3. Therefore, it is not closed under addition. (The set of polynomials of degree less than or equal to 3 is a vector space).

Q369. In R², the span of the single non-zero vector <2, 3> is:
A) The point (2,3)
B) The entire R² plane.
C) The line passing through the origin and (2,3).
D) A circle.

Answer: C
Explanation: The span of a single non-zero vector consists of all its scalar multiples (c*<2,3>). This set of points forms a line passing through the origin.

Q370. To check if a subset W is a subspace of V, one must verify:
A) Closure under addition and scalar multiplication.
B) It contains the zero vector, is closed under addition, and closed under scalar multiplication.
C) It is a smaller set than V.
D) Every vector in W is also in V.

Answer: B
Explanation: The subspace test has three conditions: must be non-empty (easiest to check by showing it has the zero vector), must be closed under vector addition, and must be closed under scalar multiplication.

Q371. The dimension of the vector space of all 2x3 matrices is:
A) 2
B) 3
C) 5
D) 6

Answer: D
Explanation: The dimension is the number of elements in a basis. A basis for M₂₃ would consist of 6 matrices, each with a '1' in one position and '0's elsewhere. Thus, the dimension is 2x3=6.

Q372. Any set of three vectors in R² must be:
A) A basis for R²
B) Linearly independent
C) Linearly dependent
D) A spanning set for R²

Answer: C
Explanation: The dimension of R² is 2. Any set containing more vectors than the dimension of the space must be linearly dependent.

Q373. An eigenvector of a matrix A corresponding to an eigenvalue λ=0 means:
A) The transformation A sends this vector to itself.
B) The transformation A sends this vector to the zero vector.
C) The eigenvalue is not valid.
D) The matrix is the zero matrix.

Answer: B
Explanation: The definition is Av=λv. If λ=0, then Av=0v=0. This means the eigenvector is in the null space of the transformation.

Q374. If v is an eigenvector of A with eigenvalue 3, what is A(2v)?
A) 2v
B) 3v
C) 5v
D) 6v

Answer: D
Explanation: A(2v) = 2(Av) because A is a linear transformation. Since Av=3v, we have 2(Av) = 2(3v) = 6v.

Q375. A function T: V->W is a linear transformation if it preserves:
A) Vector magnitudes
B) Vector addition and scalar multiplication
C) The zero vector
D) Angles between vectors

Answer: B
Explanation: The definition of a linear transformation is T(u+v)=T(u)+T(v) and T(cu)=cT(u). These are the properties of preserving vector addition and scalar multiplication.

Chapter 11: Advanced Probability Concepts (Continued)

Q376. In a standard deck of 52 cards, what is the probability of drawing a King, given that the card drawn is a face card (King, Queen, or Jack)?
A) 1/13
B) 1/3
C) 4/52
D) 12/52

Answer: B
Explanation: There are 12 face cards in total (4 Kings, 4 Queens, 4 Jacks). This is our new sample space. The number of Kings within this space is 4. P(King | Face Card) = (Favorable Outcomes) / (Total Outcomes in new sample) = 4 / 12 = 1/3.

Q377. Bayes' Theorem is used to calculate:
A) The probability of an intersection of events.
B) The probability of a union of events.
C) A posterior probability based on a prior probability and new evidence.
D) The expected value of a random variable.

Answer: C
Explanation: Bayes' Theorem provides a mathematical way to update our beliefs (prior probability) in light of new data or evidence, resulting in a revised or posterior probability.

Q378. A factory has two machines, A and B. Machine A produces 60% of items, with 2% defective. Machine B produces 40% of items, with 3% defective. What is the total probability that a randomly selected item is defective?
A) 5%
B) 2.5%
C) 2.4%
D) 2.6%

Answer: C
Explanation: Use the Law of Total Probability: P(Defective) = P(Defective|A)P(A) + P(Defective|B)P(B).
P(D) = (0.02)(0.60) + (0.03)(0.40) = 0.012 + 0.012 = 0.024 or 2.4%.

Q379. In the context of the previous question, if an item is found to be defective, what is the probability it came from Machine A?
A) 0.5
B) 0.6
C) 0.012
D) 0.4

Answer: A
Explanation: Use Bayes' Theorem: P(A|D) = [P(D|A)P(A)] / P(D).
P(A|D) = [(0.02)(0.60)] / 0.024 = 0.012 / 0.024 = 0.5 or 50%.

Q380. A variable that can take on any value in an interval is called:
A) A discrete random variable
B) A continuous random variable
C) A categorical variable
D) A parameter

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of a continuous random variable (e.g., height, time).

Q381. What is the variance of a binomial distribution with n=20 and p=0.4?
A) 8
B) 4.8
C) 12
D) 2.19

Answer: B
Explanation: Variance σ² = npq = np(1-p) = 20(0.4)(1-0.4) = 8(0.6) = 4.8.

Q382. When can the Poisson distribution be used to approximate the Binomial distribution?
A) When n is large and p is close to 0.5.
B) When n is small and p is large.
C) When n is large and p is small.
D) When np > nq.

Answer: C
Explanation: The Poisson distribution is a good approximation for the Binomial when the number of trials n is large and the probability of success p is small (often called a "rare event" scenario). A common rule of thumb is n ≥ 20 and p ≤ 0.05.

Continuing with Chapter 11: Advanced Probability Concepts

Q383. What is the key parameter for the Exponential distribution?
A) n, the number of trials
B) p, the probability of success
C) λ, the rate parameter
D) μ and σ, the mean and standard deviation

Answer: C
Explanation: The exponential distribution, which models the time between events in a Poisson process, is defined by a single parameter λ (lambda), which is the average rate of event occurrences.

Q384. A machine produces items with a 10% defect rate. What is the probability that in a sample of 10 items, exactly one is defective?
A) (0.1)¹(0.9)⁹
B) 10(0.1)¹(0.9)⁹
C) ¹⁰C₂(0.1)²(0.9)⁸
D) (0.9)¹⁰

Answer: B
Explanation: This is a binomial problem with n=10, p=0.1. P(X=1) = ¹⁰C₁ (0.1)¹ (0.9)¹⁰⁻¹ = 10(0.1)¹(0.9)⁹.

Q385. The graph of a continuous Uniform distribution f(x) from x=a to x=b looks like a:
A) Bell curve
B) Skewed curve
C) Rectangle
D) Triangle

Answer: C
Explanation: The probability density function is constant over the interval [a,b], f(x) = 1/(b-a), and zero elsewhere. This creates a rectangular shape.

Q386. The mean of an exponential distribution with rate parameter λ = 0.2 is:
A) 0.2
B) 5
C) 0.04
D) 25

Answer: B
Explanation: The mean of an exponential distribution is E(X) = 1/λ. So, E(X) = 1/0.2 = 5.

Q387. The standard normal distribution has a mean and standard deviation of:
A) μ=1, σ=0
B) μ=1, σ=1
C) μ=0, σ=1
D) μ=0, σ=0

Answer: C
Explanation: By definition, the standard normal distribution, denoted Z, is a normal distribution that has been standardized to have a mean of 0 and a standard deviation of 1.

Q388. What is the value of E(X²) if E(X) = 5 and Var(X) = 3?
A) 8
B) 16
C) 22
D) 28

Answer: D
Explanation: The computational formula for variance is Var(X) = E(X²) - [E(X)]².
3 = E(X²) - (5)² => 3 = E(X²) - 25 => E(X²) = 28.

Q389. Two events A and B are mutually exclusive. If P(A)=0.4 and P(B)=0.3, what is P(A∪B)?
A) 0.1
B) 0.12
C) 0.7
D) 0.58

Answer: C
Explanation: For mutually exclusive events, P(A∩B) = 0. The addition rule is P(A∪B) = P(A) + P(B) - P(A∩B) = 0.4 + 0.3 - 0 = 0.7.

Q390. If P(X≤k) is the cumulative distribution function (CDF), then the probability P(a < X ≤ b) is:
A) P(X≤b) / P(X≤a)
B) P(X≤b) - P(X≤a)
C) P(X≤a) - P(X≤b)
D) P(X≤b) + P(X≤a)

Answer: B
Explanation: The probability of being in an interval is the cumulative probability up to the upper bound minus the cumulative probability up to the lower bound.

Q391. The 68-95-99.7 rule for normal distributions states that 95% of data falls within how many standard deviations of the mean?
A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 1.96

Answer: B
Explanation: The empirical rule states approximately 68% of data is within ±1σ, 95% within ±2σ, and 99.7% within ±3σ of the mean.

Chapter 12: Statistical Inference and Hypothesis Testing (Continued)

Q392. What happens to the width of a confidence interval as the sample size (n) increases?
A) It increases.
B) It decreases.
C) It stays the same.
D) It becomes negative.

Answer: B
Explanation: The formula for the margin of error has √n in the denominator. As n increases, the margin of error decreases, making the confidence interval narrower and more precise.

Q393. A 99% confidence interval will be ___ a 90% confidence interval for the same data.
A) wider than
B) narrower than
C) the same width as
D) centered differently than

Answer: A
Explanation: To be more confident (99% vs 90%) that you have captured the true parameter, you need to cast a wider net. The critical value (Z* or t*) is larger for a higher confidence level, resulting in a wider interval.

Q394. In a hypothesis test, the p-value is 0.25. At a 5% significance level, this means:
A) We have strong evidence against the null hypothesis.
B) We reject the null hypothesis.
C) We fail to reject the null hypothesis.
D) The alternative hypothesis is true.

Answer: C
Explanation: The p-value (0.25) is greater than the significance level α (0.05). When p-value > α, we fail to reject the null hypothesis. There is not enough evidence to support the alternative.

Q395. A Type II error in hypothesis testing is:
A) Rejecting a true H₀
B) Failing to reject a true H₀
C) Rejecting a false H₀
D) Failing to reject a false H₀

Answer: D
Explanation: A Type II error is a "missed opportunity". It's the error of failing to detect an effect that is actually there, i.e., not rejecting the null hypothesis when it is, in fact, false.

Q396. The degrees of freedom for a t-test of a single population mean with a sample of size n=20 is:
A) 20
B) 19
C) 18
D) Not applicable

Answer: B
Explanation: For a one-sample t-test, the degrees of freedom (df) are n - 1. So, df = 20 - 1 = 19.

Q397. For a chi-square goodness-of-fit test with 5 categories, the degrees of freedom are:
A) 5
B) 4
C) 2.5
D) Dependent on sample size.

Answer: B
Explanation: For a goodness-of-fit test, df = (number of categories) - 1. So, df = 5 - 1 = 4.

Q398. If a linear regression model has an r² value of 0.64, what is the correlation coefficient, r?
A) 0.8
B) 0.41
C) 0.64
D) Cannot be determined

Answer: D
Explanation: r² = 0.64 means r = ±√0.64 = ±0.8. We know the strength of the correlation is 0.8, but without seeing the scatterplot or the sign of the slope, we cannot determine if the relationship is positive or negative.

Q399. Using the regression line ŷ = 50 - 2.5x to predict the value of y when x = 10 is an example of:
A) Interpolation (assuming 10 is within the range of data).
B) Extrapolation (assuming 10 is outside the range of data).
C) Residual analysis.
D) Calculating correlation.

Answer: A/B (Answer depends on an unstated assumption) - The best choice reflects the act itself. The action is prediction.
Let's refine the question to have one clear answer.
Q399. (Revised) The act of using a regression line to estimate a y-value for an x-value that is outside the range of the original data is called:
A) Interpolation
B) Extrapolation
C) A valid prediction
D) Standard error

Answer: B
Explanation: Extrapolation is predicting outside the observed range of x-values. It is generally unreliable as the linear trend may not continue. Interpolation is predicting within the range.

Q400. In a chi-square test for independence, the null hypothesis states that the two categorical variables are:
A) Associated
B) Dependent
C) Correlated
D) Independent

Answer: D
Explanation: The null hypothesis for this test is that there is no relationship or association between the two variables, i.e., they are independent. The alternative hypothesis is that they are dependent (associated).

Q401. If a z-test statistic for a two-tailed test is -2.15, and the critical values are ±1.96, what is the conclusion?
A) Reject H₀
B) Fail to reject H₀
C) Accept H₀
D) Reduce the sample size.

Answer: A
Explanation: The test statistic (-2.15) falls in the rejection region, as it is more extreme than the critical value of -1.96 (i.e., -2.15 < -1.96).

More Questions - Mixed Review Batch 1

Q402. (Ch 1) What is arg(1)?
A) 0
B) π
C) π/2
D) Not defined

Answer: A
Explanation: The number 1 lies on the positive real axis. Its angle with the positive real axis is 0 radians.

Q403. (Ch 2) Find the coefficient of x⁶ in the expansion of (1 - x²)⁶.
This seems too simple. Let's make it (1-x²)⁸.
Q403. (Ch 2, Revised) Find the coefficient of x⁶ in the expansion of (1 - x²)⁸.
A) 28
B) -28
C) 56
D) -56

Answer: B
Explanation: We need the term with (x²)³. This occurs when r=3 in the expansion using (y)ʳ.
The term is ⁸C₃ (1)⁸⁻³ (-x²)³ = 56 * 1 * (-x⁶) = -56x⁶. The coefficient is -56.

Q404. (Ch 3) A polynomial has a root x=2 with multiplicity 3. The equation could be:
A) (x-2)² = 0
B) x-2 = 0
C) (x-3)² = 0
D) (x-2)³(x+1) = 0

Answer: D
Explanation: A root of x=2 with multiplicity 3 means the factor (x-2) appears 3 times. Option D contains the factor (x-2)³.

Q405. (Ch 4) Solve e^(x+1) = 1.
A) x = 0
B) x = 1
C) x = -1
D) x = e

Answer: C
Explanation: For e^y = 1, y must be 0. So, we need x+1=0, which means x=-1.

Q406. (Ch 5) Find lim (h→0) [ (x+h)³ - x³ ] / h.
A) x³
B) 3x²
C) 3x
D) 0

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of the derivative of the function f(x)=x³. Using the power rule, the derivative is 3x².

Q407. (Ch 6) Evaluate ∫[0,π] sin(x) dx.
A) 0
B) 1
C) 2
D) -2

Answer: C
Explanation: ∫sin(x)dx = -cos(x). Evaluating: [-cos(x)]_[0,π] = (-cos(π)) - (-cos(0)) = (-(-1)) - (-1) = 1 + 1 = 2.

Q408. (Ch 7) Find the characteristic equation for y'' - 16y = 0.
A) m² - 16 = 0
B) m² - 16m = 0
C) m - 16 = 0
D) m² + 16 = 0

Answer: A
Explanation: y'' corresponds to m² and y corresponds to 1 (or c=-16). The y' term is missing, so there is no m term. The equation is m² - 16 = 0.

Q409. (Ch 8) What is i · k? (dot product of standard unit vectors)
A) j
B) -j
C) 1
D) 0

Answer: D
Explanation: i=<1,0,0> and k=<0,0,1>. The standard unit vectors are orthogonal. Therefore, their dot product is 0.

Q410. (Ch 9) If A = [[2, 1], [4, 2]], find its inverse.
A) [[2, -1], [-4, 2]]
B) Does not exist
C) [[1/2, -1/4], [-1, 1]]
D) [[-2, 1], [4, -2]]

Answer: B
Explanation: The determinant is det(A) = (2)(2) - (1)(4) = 4 - 4 = 0. A matrix with a zero determinant is singular and does not have an inverse.

Q411. (Ch 10) Which of the following sets could be a basis for R²?
A) {<1,1>}
B) {<1,0>, <0,1>, <1,1>}
C) {<2,3>, <4,6>}
D) {<1,2>, <3,1>}

Answer: D
Explanation: A basis for R² must contain exactly two linearly independent vectors.
A: only one vector, cannot span R².
B: three vectors, must be linearly dependent.
C: The vectors are linearly dependent (<4,6>=2*<2,3>).
D: Two vectors that are not scalar multiples of each other, so they are linearly independent and form a basis.

Q412. (Ch 11) A fair die is rolled. What is the probability of rolling a number greater than 4?
A) 1/6
B) 2/6 (1/3)
C) 3/6 (1/2)
D) 4/6 (2/3)

Answer: B
Explanation: The outcomes greater than 4 are {5, 6}. There are 2 favorable outcomes out of 6 possible outcomes. P = 2/6 = 1/3.

Q413. (Ch 12) In the equation for a regression line, ŷ = a + bx, 'b' represents the:
A) Y-intercept
B) Slope
C) Correlation coefficient
D) Predicted y value

Answer: B
Explanation: In the standard form of a linear equation, the coefficient of the independent variable x (here 'b') represents the slope of the line.

Q414. (Ch 1) Express i/(1-i) in the form a+bi.
A) 1/2 - i/2
B) -1/2 + i/2
C) 1/2 + i/2
D) -1/2 - i/2

Answer: B
Explanation: [i(1+i)]/[(1-i)(1+i)] = [i+i²]/[1²+1²] = [i-1]/2 = -1/2 + i/2.

Q415. (Ch 5) Find a critical point for the function f(x) = x³ - 3x + 5.
A) x=0
B) x=3
C) x=1 and x=-1
D) x=√3

Answer: C
Explanation: Critical points occur where f'(x)=0. f'(x) = 3x² - 3. Set to zero: 3x²-3=0 => 3x²=3 => x²=1 => x=±1.

Set 2 Questions - By Chapter
Chapter 1: Complex Numbers

Q416. The square of any purely imaginary number (bi, where b≠0) is always:
A) Positive real
B) Negative real
C) Complex
D) Zero

Answer: B
Explanation: (bi)² = b²i² = b²(-1) = -b². Since b≠0, b² is a positive real number, so -b² is a negative real number.

Q417. Find z such that 2z + z̅ = 9 - 2i.
A) 3 + 2i
B) 3 - 2i
C) 3 - i
D) 9/2 - 2i

Answer: B
Explanation: Let z=a+bi. Then z̅=a-bi.
2(a+bi) + (a-bi) = 9-2i
2a+2bi + a-bi = 9-2i
(3a) + (b)i = 9 - 2i.
Equating real and imaginary parts: 3a=9 => a=3. b=-2. So z=3-2i.

Q418. The argument of z is φ. What is the argument of 1/z?
A) φ
B) -φ
C) 1/φ
D) π-φ

Answer: B
Explanation: 1/z = z⁻¹. By De Moivre's theorem for division/powers, the argument of zⁿ is nφ. So the argument of z⁻¹ is -1*φ = -φ.

Q419. If z is a root of the polynomial equation ax³+bx²+cx+d=0 with real coefficients, then ___ must also be a root.
A) -z
B) 1/z
C) z̅
D) |z|

Answer: C
Explanation: The Complex Conjugate Root Theorem states that for polynomials with real coefficients, complex roots must occur in conjugate pairs. If z is a root, z̅ must be a root.

Q420. The inequality |z - 3| < 2 describes:
A) The exterior of a circle with center (3,0) and radius 2.
B) The interior of a circle with center (3,0) and radius 2.
C) The circle itself with center (3,0) and radius 2.
D) A line segment.

Answer: B
Explanation: The inequality describes the set of all points z whose distance from the point 3 (i.e., 3+0i) is less than 2. This is the interior of a circle (a disk without the boundary).

Q421. Evaluate |(3+4i)/(5-12i)|.
A) 1/5
B) 5/13
C) 1
D) 13/5

Answer: B
Explanation: Use the property |z₁/z₂| = |z₁|/|z₂|.
|3+4i| = √(3²+4²) = 5.
|5-12i| = √(5²+(-12)²) = √(25+144)=√169=13.
The result is 5/13.

Q422. Convert z = 4e^(iπ/3) to Cartesian form.
A) 2 + 2i√3
B) 2√3 + 2i
C) 4√3 + 4i
D) 4 + 4i√3

Answer: A
Explanation: z = 4(cos(π/3) + isin(π/3)) = 4(1/2 + i(√3/2)) = 2 + 2i√3.

Q423. Which of the following is one of the 4th roots of -1?
A) i
B) -i
C) 1/√2 + i/√2
D) 1

Answer: C
Explanation: -1 = 1(cosπ + isinπ). Roots are w_k = 1[cos((π+2kπ)/4)+isin((π+2kπ)/4)]. For k=0, w₀ = cos(π/4)+isin(π/4) = 1/√2 + i/√2.

Chapter 2: Binomial Theorem and Series Expansions

Q424. The term with the greatest coefficient in the expansion of (x+y)¹⁰ is:
A) The 1st term
B) The 11th term
C) The 6th term
D) The 5th term

Answer: C
Explanation: For (x+y)ⁿ where n is even, the binomial coefficient nCr is largest in the middle, at r=n/2. For n=10, the largest coefficient is ¹⁰C₅. This is the coefficient of the (5+1)=6th term.

Q425. If (1+kx)⁶ = 1 - 12x + ..., find the value of k.
A) -2
B) 2
C) -6
D) 6

Answer: A
Explanation: Expansion is 1 + 6(kx) + ... = 1 + 6kx + ...
Equating coefficients: 6k = -12 => k = -2.

Q426. Find the coefficient of x in the expansion of (2 - x/2)⁸.
A) -64
B) -128
C) -224
D) -4

Answer: C
Explanation: The term with x¹ is when r=1. T₂ = ⁸C₁ (2)⁸⁻¹ (-x/2)¹.
= 8 * (2⁷) * (-x/2) = 8 * 128 * (-x/2) = - (8128)/2 * x = -4128x = -512x. Wait, recheck.
T_(r+1) = ⁸Cᵣ(2)⁸⁻ʳ(-x/2)ʳ. For term in x, we need r=1.
T₂ = ⁸C₁(2)⁷(-x/2)¹ = 8 * 128 * (-1/2) * x = -4 * 128 * x = -512x. Still get -512. Let me check the options.
Maybe the question intended a different power. (2-x/2)⁶. Term is ⁶C₁(2)⁵(-x/2) = 6 * 32 * (-x/2) = -332x = -96x.
Maybe (2-x)⁸. Term is ⁸C₁(2)⁷(-x) = 8128(-x)=-1024x.
Let's stick with the original question and re-check my work very carefully.
T₂ = ⁸C₁ (2)⁷ (-x/2)¹
= 8 * (128) * (-1/2) * x
= -4 * 128 * x
= -512x.
The provided options seem to be incorrect for the question as stated. Let's create a question that results in one of the options.
Let's try to get -224. To get 224, you need 828 or 732. The term for r=3 would be ⁸C₃(2)⁵(-x/2)³ = 56 * 32 * (-x³/8) = -564x³ = -224x³. This gives a coefficient of -224 for x³. So the question likely had a typo and asked for the x³ term. Let's adjust the question.
Q426. (Revised) Find the coefficient of x³ in the expansion of (2 - x/2)⁸.
A) -64
B) -128
C) -224
D) -28

Answer: C
Explanation: The general term is T_(r+1) = ⁸Cᵣ(2)⁸⁻ʳ(-x/2)ʳ. For the term in x³, we need r=3.
T₄ = ⁸C₃ (2)⁵ (-x/2)³ = 56 * 32 * (-x³/8) = 56 * 4 * (-x³) = -224x³. The coefficient is -224.

Q427. Using the first two terms of the expansion for 1/√(4+x), approximate 1/√4.08.
A) 0.49
B) 0.495
C) 0.51
D) 0.505

Answer: B
Explanation: (4+x)⁻¹/² = 4⁻¹/²(1+x/4)⁻¹/² = (1/2)(1+x/4)⁻¹/².
≈ (1/2)[1 + (-1/2)(x/4)] = (1/2)[1 - x/8].
Here x=0.08. So, (1/2)[1 - 0.08/8] = (1/2)[1 - 0.01] = (1/2)[0.99] = 0.495.

Q428. What is the sum of the coefficients of the expansion of (3x - 2y)⁴?
A) 1
B) 81
C) 16
D) 625

Answer: A
Explanation: To find the sum of coefficients, substitute x=1 and y=1.
(3(1) - 2(1))⁴ = (3-2)⁴ = 1⁴ = 1.

Q429. The Taylor series for f(x) expanded about x=a begins f(a) + f'(a)(x-a) + ... What is the next term?
A) f''(a)(x-a)²
B) f''(a)(x-a)² / 2
C) f''(a)(x-a)² / 3!
D) f''(x)(x-a)² / 2

Answer: B
Explanation: The Taylor series term for the k-th derivative is (f^(k)(a)/k!)(x-a)^k. For k=2, this is (f''(a)/2!)(x-a)², which is f''(a)(x-a)²/2.

Q430. The Maclaurin series for ln(1+x) is valid for:
A) All real x
B) |x| < 1
C) -1 < x ≤ 1
D) x > -1

Answer: C
Explanation: The Maclaurin series for ln(1+x) converges for -1 < x < 1. At x=1, the series becomes the convergent alternating harmonic series. At x=-1, it's the divergent harmonic series. So the interval of convergence is (-1, 1].

Chapter 3: Polynomials and Rational Functions

Q431. Given P(x) = (x-1)(x-2)(x-3), what is the remainder when P(x) is divided by (x-4)?
A) 0
B) -6
C) 6
D) 24

Answer: C
Explanation: By the Remainder Theorem, the remainder is P(4).
P(4) = (4-1)(4-2)(4-3) = (3)(2)(1) = 6.

Q432. The sum of the roots of a cubic ax³+bx²+cx+d=0 is -b/a. The product of the roots is:
A) c/a
B) -c/a
C) d/a
D) -d/a

Answer: D
Explanation: For a cubic, the product of the roots (r₁r₂r₃) is equal to -d/a.

Q433. Find all real roots of the equation x⁴ - 81 = 0.
A) 3
B) -3
C) 3, -3
D) 3, -3, 3i, -3i

Answer: C
Explanation: x⁴ - 81 = (x² - 9)(x² + 9) = 0.
x² - 9 = 0 => (x-3)(x+3)=0 => x=3, x=-3.
x² + 9 = 0 => x²=-9 => x=±3i.
The question asks for only the real roots.

Q434. The vertical asymptote of y = x/(x²-4) are:
A) x=2
B) x=-2
C) x=2 and x=-2
D) y=0

Answer: C
Explanation: Vertical asymptotes occur where the denominator is zero. x²-4=0 => (x-2)(x+2)=0 => x=2 and x=-2.

Q435. Find the x-intercepts of y = (x² - 5x + 6) / (x + 1).
A) -1
B) 2 and 3
C) -2 and -3
D) 6

Answer: B
Explanation: X-intercepts occur where the numerator is zero. x²-5x+6=0 => (x-2)(x-3)=0. So x=2 and x=3.

Q436. When performing synthetic division on x³-8 by x-2, the initial setup line is:
A) 2 | 1 0 0 -8
B) -2 | 1 -8
C) 2 | 1 -8
D) 2 | 1 0 -8

Answer: A
Explanation: The polynomial x³-8 is x³ + 0x² + 0x - 8. You must include zero coefficients for the missing terms. The coefficients are 1, 0, 0, -8. The divisor x-2 means c=2.

Chapter 4: Logarithmic and Exponential Functions (Continued II)

Q437. What is log₅(1)?
A) 5
B) 1
C) 0
D) undefined

Answer: C
Explanation: For any valid base b, b⁰ = 1. Therefore, log_b(1) = 0.

Q438. The graph of an exponential function f(x) = b^x (where b > 0, b ≠ 1) always passes through the point:
A) (0, 0)
B) (1, 1)
C) (1, 0)
D) (0, 1)

Answer: D
Explanation: For any base b, b⁰ = 1. So, when x=0, y=1. The graph always contains the y-intercept (0, 1).

Q439. Solve for x: 2 log(x) = log(9).
A) x = 3
B) x = 4.5
C) x = 81
D) x = 9

Answer: A
Explanation: Using the power rule, log(x²) = log(9). If the logs are equal, their arguments are equal: x² = 9. Since the argument of a log must be positive, x = 3 (we reject x=-3).

Q440. Which expression is undefined in the real number system?
A) log(100)
B) ln(e)
C) log(0)
D) log(0.5)

Answer: C
Explanation: The argument of a logarithm must be a positive number. The logarithm of zero or a negative number is undefined in the real number system.

Q441. An earthquake measures 6 on the Richter scale, and another measures 4. The intensity of the first is how many times greater than the second? (Richter scale is logarithmic base 10).
A) 2
B) 1.5
C) 20
D) 100

Answer: D
Explanation: The Richter scale is a measure of magnitude M=log(I/I₀). A difference of 1 on the scale means a 10-fold increase in intensity (I). A difference of 2 (from 4 to 6) means a 10² = 100-fold increase in intensity.

Q442. If log_a(x)=2 and log_a(y)=3, find log_a(xy²).
A) 8
B) 12
C) 7
D) 9

Answer: A
Explanation: log_a(xy²) = log_a(x) + log_a(y²) = log_a(x) + 2log_a(y) = 2 + 2(3) = 2 + 6 = 8.

Q443. The decay model N(t) = N₀e^(-λt) is given. What does λ represent?
A) The half-life
B) The initial amount
C) The amount remaining
D) The decay constant

Answer: D
Explanation: λ (lambda) is the positive constant that determines the rate of decay. It is related to the half-life by t½ = ln(2)/λ.

Chapter 5: Limits, Continuity, and Differentiation (Continued II)

Q444. lim (x→-∞) [x³ + 2x - 1]
A) ∞
B) -∞
C) 0
D) Does not exist

Answer: B
Explanation: The end behavior of a polynomial is determined by its leading term. As x becomes a large negative number, x³ also becomes a large negative number.

Q445. What is the derivative of y = (x² + 1)⁻¹?
A) -1(x²+1)⁻²
B) -2x(x²+1)⁻²
C) (x²+1)⁻²
D) -2x

Answer: B
Explanation: Using the chain rule: y' = -1(x²+1)⁻² * d/dx(x²+1) = -1(x²+1)⁻² * (2x) = -2x(x²+1)⁻².

Q446. The derivative of y = ln(kx) for a constant k is:
A) k/x
B) 1/(kx)
C) 1/x
D) ln(k) + 1/x

Answer: C
Explanation: Method 1: Use log properties. y = ln(k) + ln(x). The derivative of ln(k) (a constant) is 0, and derivative of ln(x) is 1/x. So y'=1/x.
Method 2: Chain rule. y' = (1/kx) * d/dx(kx) = (1/kx) * k = 1/x.

Q447. At a point of inflection, which of the following MUST be true?
A) The first derivative is zero.
B) The second derivative is zero.
C) The concavity of the graph changes.
D) The function is at a maximum or minimum.

Answer: C
Explanation: This is the definition of a point of inflection. The second derivative being zero is a necessary condition (for smooth functions), but not sufficient; the concavity must actually change sign.

Q448. A particle's position is given by s(t) = t³ - 6t² + 9t. When is the particle at rest?
A) t = 1 and t = 3
B) t = 2
C) t = 0
D) Never

Answer: A
Explanation: The particle is at rest when its velocity is zero. Velocity v(t) = s'(t) = 3t² - 12t + 9.
Set v(t)=0: 3(t² - 4t + 3) = 0 => 3(t-1)(t-3) = 0. So t=1 and t=3.

Q449. For the function in the previous question, what is the acceleration at t=2?
A) 0
B) -6
C) 6
D) 12

Answer: A
Explanation: Acceleration a(t) = v'(t) = s''(t) = 6t - 12.
a(2) = 6(2) - 12 = 12 - 12 = 0.

Q450. Find the derivative of y = x ln(x).
A) 1
B) ln(x)
C) 1 + ln(x)
D) 1/x

Answer: C
Explanation: Using the product rule: y' = (1)(ln x) + (x)(1/x) = ln(x) + 1.

Q451. Find dy/dt if y = x² and x = 2t + 1 (Related Rates/Chain Rule).
A) 4t+2
B) 2x
C) 2(4t+2)
D) 4(2t+1)

Answer: D
Explanation: By the chain rule, dy/dt = (dy/dx)(dx/dt).
dy/dx = 2x. dx/dt = 2.
dy/dt = (2x)(2) = 4x. Substitute x=2t+1 back in: dy/dt = 4(2t+1).

Q452. The first derivative test for a local maximum at x=c requires the sign of f'(x) to change from...
A) positive to negative.
B) negative to positive.
C) positive to positive.
D) negative to negative.

Answer: A
Explanation: A local maximum occurs where the function stops increasing (f'>0) and starts decreasing (f'<0).

Chapter 6: Integration (Continued II)

Q453. If F(x) = ∫[1,x] t² dt, what is F'(x)?
A) x²
B) x²/2
C) 2x
D) x² - 1

Answer: A
Explanation: This is an application of the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus, Part 1: d/dx [∫[a,x] f(t)dt] = f(x). Here, f(t)=t², so F'(x) = x².

Q454. The expression ∫ f(x) dx is called:
A) A definite integral
B) A proper integral
C) An indefinite integral
D) An improper integral

Answer: C
Explanation: An integral without limits of integration is an indefinite integral, and its result is a family of functions (an antiderivative + C).

Q455. Find the average value of f(x) = 3x² on the interval [0, 2].
A) 8
B) 4
C) 2
D) 12

Answer: B
Explanation: Avg Value = (1/2) ∫[0,2] 3x² dx = (1/2) [x³]_[0,2] = (1/2)(2³ - 0³) = (1/2)(8) = 4.

Q456. Evaluate ∫[a,b] f(x)dx, if the area under y=f(x) from a to b is 10 and the area above y=f(x) (below x-axis) is 3 within the same interval.
Question is ambiguous. Rephrase.
Q456. (Revised) From x=a to x=b, the area of the region between y=f(x) and the x-axis that is above the axis is 10, and the area of the region below the axis is 3. Find ∫[a,b] f(x) dx.
A) 13
B) 7
C) -3
D) 10

Answer: B
Explanation: The definite integral represents net signed area. Area above is positive (+10), area below is negative (-3). The net area is 10 - 3 = 7.

Q457. Which integral represents the volume when the region between y=x² and y=√x is rotated about the x-axis?
A) ∫[0,1] π (√x - x²)² dx
B) ∫[0,1] π (x - x⁴) dx
C) ∫[0,1] 2π (√x - x²) dx
D) ∫[0,1] π (x² - √x) dx

Answer: B
Explanation: Using the Washer Method. First find intersection points: x²=√x => x⁴=x => x⁴-x=0 => x(x³-1)=0. Intersections at x=0, x=1. In [0,1], √x is the upper curve (outer radius R(x)) and x² is the lower curve (inner radius r(x)).
Volume = ∫[0,1] π([R(x)]² - [r(x)]²)dx = ∫[0,1] π((√x)² - (x²)²)dx = ∫[0,1] π(x - x⁴)dx.

Q458. Use integration by parts to evaluate ∫ ln(x) dx.
A) x ln(x) - x + C
B) 1/x + C
C) x ln(x) + C
D) ln(x)/x + C

Answer: A
Explanation: Let u = ln(x) and dv = dx. Then du = (1/x)dx and v = x.
∫ln(x)dx = uv - ∫vdu = ln(x)x - ∫x(1/x)dx = xln(x) - ∫1dx = xln(x) - x + C.

Q459. The partial fraction decomposition of 1 / (x²(x-1)) is of the form:
A) A/x + B/(x-1)
B) A/x² + B/(x-1)
C) Ax+B / x² + C/(x-1)
D) A/x + B/x² + C/(x-1)

Answer: D
Explanation: For a repeated linear factor like x², you need a term for each power up to the multiplicity. So you need a term for x and a term for x². The distinct linear factor (x-1) gets its own term.

Chapter 7: Ordinary Differential Equations (Continued II)

Q460. The general solution of dy/dx = k is:
A) y = kx + C
B) y = kx²/2 + C
C) y = e^(kx) + C
D) y = ln|x| + C

Answer: A
Explanation: This is a simple separation of variables: dy = k dx. Integrating both sides gives y = kx + C, the equation of a family of lines with slope k.

Q461. The standard form for a first-order linear DE solved with an integrating factor is y' + P(x)y = Q(x). The integrating factor is e^(∫P(x)dx). After multiplying by the IF, the left side of the equation becomes:
A) d/dx [y]
B) d/dx [IF * Q(x)]
C) d/dx [IF * y]
D) IF * y'

Answer: C
Explanation: The entire point of the integrating factor is that it turns the left side of the equation into the result of the product rule for the derivative of (IF * y). So d/dx [IF * y] = IF*y' + (IF)'*y.

Q462. What type of DE is y' = (x²+y²)/xy?
A) Separable
B) Linear
C) Homogeneous
D) Second-order

Answer: C
Explanation: A DE is homogeneous if it can be written in the form y' = f(y/x). Here, divide top and bottom by x²: y' = (1 + (y/x)²)/(y/x). Since it's a function of (y/x), it is homogeneous and can be solved with the substitution v=y/x.

Q463. An initial condition for a first-order DE specifies:
A) The slope of the solution at a point.
B) The area under the solution curve.
C) The value of the solution (y) at a specific point (x).
D) The concavity of the solution.

Answer: C
Explanation: An initial condition, like y(x₀)=y₀, provides a single point that the solution curve must pass through. This allows finding the specific value of the constant C in the general solution.

Q464. If the characteristic equation am²+bm+c=0 for a damped harmonic oscillator has two distinct negative real roots, the system is:
A) Underdamped (oscillates)
B) Critically damped
C) Overdamped (does not oscillate)
D) Undamped

Answer: C
Explanation: Distinct real roots correspond to an overdamped system. The solution is y = C₁e^(m₁x)+C₂e^(m₂x), where m₁ and m₂ are negative. This is a sum of two decaying exponentials, which approaches zero without oscillating.

Chapter 8: Vectors in Two and Three Dimensions (Continued II)

Q465. Two vectors are collinear if they are:
A) Perpendicular
B) Parallel
C) Have the same magnitude
D) Both start at the origin

Answer: B
Explanation: Collinear means the vectors lie on the same line if placed tip-to-tail. This is equivalent to them being parallel (one is a scalar multiple of the other).

Q466. What is the value of i × i?
A) 1
B) i
C) 0 (the zero vector)
D) -1

Answer: C
Explanation: The cross product of any vector with itself (or any parallel vector) is the zero vector, because the angle between them is 0 and sin(0)=0.

Q467. The line given by r=<1,1,1> + t<2,3,4> and the plane 2x+3y+4z=5 are:
A) Parallel
B) Perpendicular
C) Neither parallel nor perpendicular
D) The line lies in the plane.

Answer: B
Explanation: A line is perpendicular to a plane if its direction vector is parallel to the plane's normal vector. The line's direction vector is v=<2,3,4>. The plane's normal vector is n=<2,3,4>. Since v=n, they are parallel, and thus the line is perpendicular to the plane.

Q468. If u=<a,b,c>, what is u · u?
A) 0
B) 1
C) a+b+c
D) |u|²

Answer: D
Explanation: u·u = aa + bb + c*c = a²+b²+c². The magnitude squared is |u|² = (√(a²+b²+c²))² = a²+b²+c². So u·u = |u|².

Q469. Find the parametric equations for the line segment starting at P(1,2) and ending at Q(4,6).
A) x=1+3t, y=2+4t, for 0≤t≤1
B) x=4+t, y=6+2t, for 0≤t≤1
C) x=1+4t, y=2+6t, for 0≤t≤1
D) x=3t, y=4t, for 0≤t≤1

Answer: A
Explanation: A point on the line is P(1,2). The direction vector is PQ = Q-P = <4-1, 6-2> = <3,4>.
The equation of the line is r = <1,2>+t<3,4>.
The parametric equations are x=1+3t, y=2+4t.
At t=0, we are at (1,2) = P. At t=1, we are at (1+3, 2+4) = (4,6) = Q. So the parameter range 0≤t≤1 defines the segment.

More Questions - Mixed Review Batch 2

Q470. (Ch 9) A matrix that does not have an inverse is called:
A) Singular
B) Non-singular
C) Symmetric
D) A zero matrix

Answer: A
Explanation: A square matrix is called singular if its determinant is 0, which is the condition for it not having a multiplicative inverse.

Q471. (Ch 10) A linear transformation T maps the basis vectors of R² as T(i)=<2,3> and T(j)=<0,1>. What is the matrix representation of T?
A) [[2, 3], [0, 1]]
B) [[2, 0], [3, 1]]
C) [[2, 0], [1, 3]]
D) [[3, 1], [2, 0]]

Answer: B
Explanation: The columns of the matrix for a linear transformation are the images of the basis vectors. The first column is T(i)=<2,3> and the second column is T(j)=<0,1>. The matrix is [[2, 0], [3, 1]].

Q472. (Ch 11) On any given day, the probability of rain is 0.4. What is the probability that it does not rain for three consecutive days, assuming independence?
A) 0.064
B) 0.216
C) 0.6
D) 0.4

Answer: B
Explanation: The probability of not raining on one day is 1 - 0.4 = 0.6. The probability of this happening for three independent days is (0.6) * (0.6) * (0.6) = (0.6)³ = 0.216.

Q473. (Ch 12) A study is conducted to see if a new drug lowers blood pressure. What is the most appropriate alternative hypothesis (H₁)?
A) H₁: μ = 120 (where μ is average blood pressure)
B) H₁: μ ≠ 120
C) H₁: μ > 120
D) H₁: μ < μ₀ (where μ₀ is average pressure before the drug)

Answer: D
Explanation: The research goal is to see if the drug lowers blood pressure. This directional goal translates into a one-tailed alternative hypothesis, stating that the mean pressure with the drug (μ) is less than the original mean pressure (μ₀).

Q474. (Ch 1) What is |e^(ix)|?
A) 1
B) e
C) x
D) cos(x)

Answer: A
Explanation: e^(ix) = cos(x) + i sin(x). Its modulus is √(cos²x + sin²x) = √1 = 1.

Q475. (Ch 2) In the expansion of (a-2b)ⁿ, the sum of the coefficients is 1. Find n.
Question is problematic as (1-2)ⁿ = (-1)ⁿ which can be 1 or -1. Rephrase to be unambiguous.
Q475. (Ch 2, Revised) In the expansion of (3x - 2)ⁿ, the sum of the coefficients is 1. What can be said about n?
A) n is any positive integer.
B) n is any even integer.
C) n is any odd integer.
D) n must be 1.

Answer: A
Explanation: The sum of coefficients is found by setting x=1. (3(1) - 2)ⁿ = (1)ⁿ = 1. This is true for any power n.

Q476. (Ch 3) If P(x) is a polynomial of degree 4, what is the end behavior if the leading coefficient is positive?
A) Rises left, falls right
B) Falls left, rises right
C) Rises left, rises right
D) Falls left, falls right

Answer: C
Explanation: A positive leading coefficient means the graph rises on the right. An even degree (4) means the left end does the same thing as the right end. So, it rises left and rises right (like a 'U').

Q477. (Ch 4) What is logₓ(x)?
A) 1
B) 0
C) x
D) 10

Answer: A
Explanation: To what power must x be raised to get x? The answer is 1. (x¹=x).

Q478. (Ch 5) Differentiate y = √(x²+9).
A) 1 / (2√(x²+9))
B) x / √(x²+9)
C) 2x / √(x²+9)
D) x √(x²+9)

Answer: B
Explanation: y=(x²+9)¹/². Use chain rule: y' = (1/2)(x²+9)⁻¹/² * (2x) = x(x²+9)⁻¹/² = x / √(x²+9).

Q479. (Ch 6) Evaluate ∫[0,1] e^x dx.
A) e
B) e-1
C) 1
D) 0

Answer: B
Explanation: ∫e^x dx = e^x. Evaluating: [e^x]_[0,1] = e¹ - e⁰ = e - 1.

Q480. (Ch 7) A differential equation whose general solution is y=Ce^x is:
A) y' = y
B) y' = -y
C) y' = x
D) y'' = y

Answer: A
Explanation: If y=Ce^x, then y'=Ce^x. Thus, y'=y.

Q481. (Ch 8) What are the direction cosines for the vector v = <1, 2, 2>?
A) <1/9, 2/9, 2/9>
B) <1, 2, 2>
C) <1/3, 2/3, 2/3>
D) <1/√3, 2/√3, 2/√3>

Answer: C
Explanation: The magnitude is |v| = √(1²+2²+2²)=√9=3. The direction cosines are the components of the unit vector in that direction. The unit vector is <1/3, 2/3, 2/3>.

Q482. (Ch 9) If det(A)=4 and det(B)=5, what is det(A^T B)?
A) 9
B) 20
C) -1
D) 4/5

Answer: B
Explanation: det(A^T) = det(A) = 4. det(AB) = det(A)det(B). Therefore, det(A^T B) = det(A^T)det(B) = 4 * 5 = 20.

Q483. (Ch 10) Is the set of all vectors <x,y> where x ≥ 0 a subspace of R²?
A) Yes
B) No, it does not contain the zero vector.
C) No, it is not closed under addition.
D) No, it is not closed under scalar multiplication.

Answer: D
Explanation: The set contains the zero vector <0,0> and is closed under addition. However, it is not closed under scalar multiplication. Take vector <1,1> from the set. Multiplying by scalar c=-1 gives <-1,-1>, which is not in the set because the x-component is negative.

Q484. (Ch 11) For a standard normal distribution, P(Z < 0) is:
A) 0
B) 1
C) 0.5
D) Not determinable.

Answer: C
Explanation: The standard normal curve is symmetric about its mean, Z=0. The total area under the curve is 1, so the area to the left of 0 must be exactly half, which is 0.5.

Q485. (Ch 12) In regression analysis, a single data point's residual is:
A) Its predicted y-value (ŷ)
B) The difference between the actual y and the predicted y (y - ŷ)
C) The difference between its x-value and the mean x-value.
D) The square of the error.

Answer: B
Explanation: The residual for a point is its vertical distance from the regression line, representing the prediction error for that specific point. It is calculated as actual - predicted, or y - ŷ.

Set 3 Questions - By Chapter
Chapter 1: Complex Numbers

Q486. Find z₁/z₂ if z₁=10e^(iπ/2) and z₂=5e^(iπ/4).
A) 2e^(iπ/4)
B) 2e^(i3π/4)
C) 50e^(i3π/4)
D) 2e^(iπ/2)

Answer: A
Explanation: To divide complex numbers in exponential form, divide the moduli and subtract the exponents (which represent the arguments). z₁/z₂ = (10/5)e^(i(π/2 - π/4)) = 2e^(iπ/4).

Q487. A complex number is purely real if and only if:
A) z = z̅
B) z = -z̅
C) |z| = 1
D) Re(z) = Im(z)

Answer: A
Explanation: Let z = a+bi. If it is purely real, b=0, so z=a. Its conjugate z̅ is also a. So z=z̅. Conversely, if z=z̅, then a+bi = a-bi => 2bi=0 => b=0.

Q488. The complex number corresponding to the Cartesian point (0, -4) is:
A) 4
B) -4
C) 4i
D) -4i

Answer: D
Explanation: A point (a, b) corresponds to the complex number a + bi. So, (0, -4) corresponds to 0 + (-4)i = -4i.

Q489. Simplify: (2-i)²
A) 3
B) 5
C) 3 - 4i
D) 5 - 4i

Answer: C
Explanation: (2-i)² = 2² - 2(2)(i) + i² = 4 - 4i + (-1) = 3 - 4i.

Q490. What is the value of |i|?
A) 0
B) 1
C) -1
D) i

Answer: B
Explanation: i = 0+1i. |i| = √(0² + 1²) = √1 = 1.

Q491. If the product of two complex numbers is a real number, the two numbers must be:
A) Equal
B) Additive inverses
C) Multiplicative inverses
D) Not necessarily related in any special way.

Answer: D
Explanation: While complex conjugates have a real product, other pairs do too. For example (1+i)(2-2i) = 2-2i+2i-2i² = 2+2=4, which is real. But (1+i) and (2-2i) are not conjugates. So there's no mandatory relationship.

Q492. What are the solutions to z³ = 1?
A) 1, i, -i
B) 1, -1, i
C) 1, (-1+i√3)/2, (-1-i√3)/2
D) 1, 1/2+i√3/2, 1/2-i√3/2

Answer: C
Explanation: These are the three cube roots of unity. 1 is the principal root. The other two are complex conjugates found using the root formula or knowing that the roots are 1, ω, and ω².

Chapter 2: Binomial Theorem and Series Expansions

Q493. ⁰C₀ = ?
A) 0
B) 1
C) undefined
D) 0!

Answer: B
Explanation: nCn = 1 and nC0 = 1 for any non-negative integer n. By definition, 0! = 1. So ⁰C₀ = 0!/(0!0!) = 1/1 = 1.

Q494. The expansion of (1+x)ⁿ terminates (is finite) if and only if n is:
A) any real number
B) negative
C) a fraction
D) a positive integer or zero

Answer: D
Explanation: The binomial theorem gives a finite series of n+1 terms only when the power n is a non-negative integer. For all other real number powers, the expansion is an infinite series.

Q495. To approximate 1/(1.05)², you would use the binomial expansion of:
A) (1+0.05)²
B) (1-0.05)²
C) (1+0.05)⁻²
D) (1-0.05)⁻²

Answer: C
Explanation: 1/(1.05)² can be rewritten as (1.05)⁻² which is (1 + 0.05)⁻².

Q496. What is the fourth term in the Maclaurin series for e^x?
A) x³/3
B) x⁴/4
C) x³/3!
D) x⁴/4!

Answer: C
Explanation: The series is 1 + x + x²/2! + x³/3! + ... The first term is 1, second is x, third is x²/2!, fourth is x³/3!.

Chapter 3: Polynomials and Rational Functions

Q497. The horizontal asymptote of f(x) = (8x³ - ...)/(2x³ + ...) is:
A) y = 8
B) y = 2
C) y = 4
D) y = 0

Answer: C
Explanation: When the degrees of the numerator and denominator are equal, the horizontal asymptote is the ratio of their leading coefficients. Here, it's y = 8/2 = 4.

Q498. If x=c is a vertical asymptote of y=f(x), then as x approaches c, |f(x)| approaches:
A) 0
B) 1
C) ∞
D) c

Answer: C
Explanation: At a vertical asymptote, the function value increases or decreases without bound, heading towards positive or negative infinity. The absolute value |f(x)| therefore approaches infinity.

Q499. The polynomial P(x)=x⁵+3x³+2x has how many real roots?
A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 5

Answer: A
Explanation: P(x) = x(x⁴+3x²+2). One real root is x=0. The other factor is a "quadratic in disguise". Let u=x². Then u²+3u+2=0 => (u+1)(u+2)=0. So u=-1 or u=-2. This means x²=-1 or x²=-2. Both of these give complex roots (x=±i, x=±i√2). Thus, there is only one real root, x=0.

Q500. A polynomial with real coefficients has roots 1, 2, and 3+i. What must be the minimum degree of the polynomial?
A) 3
B) 4
C) 5
D) 6

Answer: B
Explanation: Because the coefficients are real, the complex root 3+i must be paired with its conjugate, 3-i. This gives a total of four roots: 1, 2, 3+i, and 3-i. Therefore, the minimum degree is 4.
