Chapter 5: Limits, Continuity, and Differentiation (Continued III)

Q501. lim (x→3⁻) (x-3) / |x-3|
A) 1
B) -1
C) 0
D) Does not exist

Answer: B
Explanation: As x approaches 3 from the left (x→3⁻), x is slightly less than 3, so (x-3) is a small negative number. Therefore, |x-3| = -(x-3). The expression becomes (x-3)/(-(x-3)) = -1.

Q502. The derivative is undefined for the function f(x)=|x| at which point?
A) x = 1
B) x = -1
C) x = 0
D) It is defined everywhere.

Answer: C
Explanation: The graph of f(x)=|x| has a sharp corner (a "cusp") at x=0. The slope is -1 from the left and +1 from the right. Since the left and right derivatives are not equal, the derivative is undefined at x=0.

Q503. A rectangle has a fixed perimeter of 20 units. What is the maximum possible area?
A) 20
B) 25
C) 30
D) 100

Answer: B
Explanation: Let length be L and width be W. 2L+2W=20 => L+W=10 => W=10-L. Area A = LW = L(10-L) = 10L - L². To maximize, find A'(L)=0. A'(L) = 10 - 2L. 10-2L=0 => L=5. If L=5, W=5. The shape is a square. Area = 55=25.

Q504. Differentiate y = tan⁻¹(x).
A) sec²(x)
B) 1 / (1+x²)
C) -1 / (1+x²)
D) 1 / √(1-x²)

Answer: B
Explanation: This is a standard derivative rule for inverse trigonometric functions: d/dx(arctan(x)) = 1 / (1+x²).

Q505. Find all critical points of f(x) = x e^x.
A) x = 0
B) x = 1
C) x = -1
D) x = 0 and x = -1

Answer: C
Explanation: Use the product rule: f'(x) = (1)e^x + (x)e^x = e^x(1+x). Since e^x is never zero, f'(x)=0 only when 1+x=0, which means x=-1.

Q506. If the acceleration of a particle is a(t)=6t and v(0)=2, find the velocity v(t).
A) v(t) = 3t²
B) v(t) = 3t² + 2
C) v(t) = 6
D) v(t) = 6t² + 2

Answer: B
Explanation: Velocity is the integral of acceleration: v(t) = ∫6t dt = 3t² + C. Use the initial condition v(0)=2 to find C. v(0) = 3(0)² + C = 2 => C=2. So, v(t) = 3t² + 2.

Q507. A function is said to be differentiable at x=c if...
A) It is continuous at x=c.
B) The limit lim (h→0) [f(c+h)-f(c)]/h exists.
C) The tangent line at x=c is horizontal.
D) The graph is smooth at x=c.

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of the derivative at a point. If this limit exists, the function is differentiable there. While smoothness is a consequence and continuity is a necessary condition, this is the formal definition.

Chapter 6: Integration (Continued III)

Q508. Evaluate ∫ tan(x) dx.
A) sec²(x) + C
B) ln|sec(x)| + C
C) ln|sin(x)| + C
D) sec(x)tan(x) + C

Answer: B
Explanation: ∫ tan(x)dx = ∫ (sin(x)/cos(x))dx. Use u-sub with u=cos(x), du=-sin(x)dx. Integral becomes ∫ -du/u = -ln|u|+C = -ln|cos(x)|+C. Using log properties, this is ln|cos(x)|⁻¹ + C = ln|sec(x)| + C.

Q509. ∫[a,b] f(x)dx = -∫[b,a] f(x)dx. This property means that:
A) The integral is always negative.
B) Reversing the limits of integration negates the value of the integral.
C) The area is always positive.
D) The function must be odd.

Answer: B
Explanation: This is a fundamental property of definite integrals. Swapping the upper and lower limits of integration changes the sign of the result.

Q510. The value of ∫[ -2, 2 ] x³ dx is:
A) 8
B) 16
C) 4
D) 0

Answer: D
Explanation: The function f(x)=x³ is an odd function (f(-x) = -f(x)). The integral of an odd function over a symmetric interval [-a, a] is always zero, because the positive and negative areas cancel out perfectly.

Q511. Which integral gives the length of the arc of the curve y=f(x) from x=a to x=b?
A) ∫[a,b] √(1 + [f'(x)]²) dx
B) ∫[a,b] √(1 + [f(x)]²) dx
C) ∫[a,b] 2π f(x) dx
D) ∫[a,b] |f(x)| dx

Answer: A
Explanation: This is the standard formula for arc length in Cartesian coordinates, derived from the Pythagorean theorem on an infinitesimal segment of the curve.

Q512. Use partial fractions to evaluate ∫ 1 / (x² - 4) dx.
A) (1/4) ln|(x-2)/(x+2)| + C
B) (1/2) ln|(x-2)/(x+2)| + C
C) ln|x-2| - ln|x+2| + C
D) arctan(x/2) + C

Answer: A
Explanation: 1/(x²-4) = A/(x-2) + B/(x+2). A=1/4, B=-1/4. The integral becomes (1/4)∫(1/(x-2) - 1/(x+2))dx = (1/4)[ln|x-2| - ln|x+2|] + C = (1/4)ln|(x-2)/(x+2)| + C.

Q513. The Cylindrical Shells method for finding volume is often useful when:
A) Rotating a region about the x-axis.
B) The function is easily solved for x in terms of y.
C) Rotating about an axis requires integrating with respect to the other variable (e.g. rotating about y-axis, integrating dx).
D) The solid has a hole in it.

Answer: C
Explanation: The Washer method would require integrating dy in this case, which might be hard if the functions are difficult to express as x=g(y). The Shells method allows you to integrate with respect to x for a y-axis rotation, which can be much simpler.

Chapter 7: Ordinary Differential Equations (Continued III)

Q514. The equation y' + xy² = 0 is best described as:
A) Linear
B) Separable
C) Second-order
D) Homogeneous with constant coefficients

Answer: B
Explanation: It is not linear because of the y² term. It is first-order. It can be separated: dy/y² = -x dx.

Q515. What kind of solution to a DE is y=0?
A) A general solution
B) A particular solution
C) A trivial solution
D) Not a solution

Answer: C
Explanation: The solution y=0 is often a valid solution, especially for homogeneous equations, and is referred to as the trivial solution.

Q516. Find the integrating factor for dy/dx - 3y = e^x.
A) e³
B) e^(3x)
C) e^(-3)
D) e^(-3x)

Answer: D
Explanation: The equation is in the form y' + P(x)y = Q(x), with P(x)=-3. IF = e^(∫-3 dx) = e^(-3x).

Q517. The general solution of y'' + 4y = 0 is y = C₁cos(2x)+C₂sin(2x). If y(0)=1 and y'(0)=6, find C₁.
A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 6

Answer: A
Explanation: Use the first condition y(0)=1. y(0) = C₁cos(0) + C₂sin(0) = C₁(1) + C₂(0) = C₁. Therefore, C₁=1.

Q518. Using the previous question's info, find C₂.
A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 6

Answer: C
Explanation: First find y'. y'(x) = -2C₁sin(2x) + 2C₂cos(2x).
Use y'(0)=6: y'(0) = -2C₁sin(0) + 2C₂cos(0) = 0 + 2C₂(1) = 2C₂.
So 2C₂ = 6 => C₂ = 3.

Q519. The Wave Equation, which describes the propagation of waves, is an example of a:
A) Linear first-order ODE
B) Non-linear ODE
C) Partial Differential Equation
D) System of ODEs

Answer: C
Explanation: The Wave Equation (∂²u/∂t² = c² ∂²u/∂x²) involves partial derivatives because the wave's displacement u depends on both position x and time t.

Chapter 8: Vectors in Two and Three Dimensions (Continued III)

Q520. If u = <1,-2,3> and v = <-3,6,-9>, the vectors are:
A) Orthogonal
B) Collinear (parallel)
C) Unit vectors
D) Skew

Answer: B
Explanation: Notice that v = -3 * <1,-2,3> = -3u. Since one vector is a scalar multiple of the other, they are collinear (parallel).

Q521. Find a vector perpendicular to both u=i+j and v=i-j.
A) k
B) 2i
C) 2k
D) -2k

Answer: D
Explanation: The cross product u×v is perpendicular to both u and v.
u×v = | i j k | | 1 1 0 | | 1 -1 0 | = i(0-0) - j(0-0) + k(-1-1) = -2k.

Q522. Find the equation of the plane that contains the point (3,2,1) and is perpendicular to the vector <1,1,1>.
A) x+y+z = 6
B) 3x+2y+z = 1
C) x+y+z = 0
D) The plane does not exist.

Answer: A
Explanation: The normal vector is n=<1,1,1>. The equation is 1(x-3) + 1(y-2) + 1(z-1) = 0 => x-3+y-2+z-1=0 => x+y+z=6.

Q523. The gradient of a scalar field, ∇φ, is a:
A) Scalar field
B) Vector field
C) Constant
D) Matrix

Answer: B
Explanation: The gradient operation takes a scalar field (like temperature) and produces a vector field that points in the direction of the steepest increase of that scalar.

Q524. The line segment from (1,1) to (5,4) is represented by r(t) = (1-t)<1,1> + t<5,4> for:
A) t in (-∞, ∞)
B) t > 0
C) t = 0 and t = 1
D) t in [0, 1]

Answer: D
Explanation: This is another way to write a line segment. When t=0, r(0)=<1,1>. When t=1, r(1)=<5,4>. As t varies from 0 to 1, the point r(t) traces the line segment between them.

Chapter 9: Matrices and Determinants (Continued II)

Q525. A scalar matrix is a special type of:
A) Identity matrix
B) Zero matrix
C) Diagonal matrix
D) Symmetric matrix

Answer: C
Explanation: A diagonal matrix has non-zero elements only on the main diagonal. A scalar matrix is a diagonal matrix where all those diagonal elements are equal (e.g., 5I).

Q526. Find the product [[1, 2]] * [[3], [4]].
A) [11]
B) [1, 8]
C) [[3, 2], [4, 8]]
D) Not defined

Answer: A
Explanation: A is a 1x2 matrix, B is a 2x1 matrix. The product is defined and will be a 1x1 matrix. The single element is (13 + 24) = 3 + 8 = 11.

Q527. If you interchange two rows of a square matrix A to get matrix B, then det(B) = ?
A) det(A)
B) -det(A)
C) 1/det(A)
D) 2det(A)

Answer: B
Explanation: Interchanging any two rows (or columns) of a matrix negates its determinant.

Q528. Find the adjugate (adjoint) of the matrix A = [[1,2],[3,4]].
A) [[4, -2], [-3, 1]]
B) [[4, -3], [-2, 1]]
C) [[-4, 2], [3, -1]]
D) [[1, 3], [2, 4]]

Answer: A
Explanation: The matrix of cofactors is [[4, -3], [-2, 1]]. The adjugate is the transpose of the cofactor matrix, which is [[4, -2], [-3, 1]]. This is the matrix used in the inverse formula before multiplying by 1/det(A).

Q529. A system of 3 linear equations in 3 unknowns has a unique solution. Geometrically, this means the three planes:
A) Intersect in a single line.
B) Are parallel.
C) Intersect at a single point.
D) Are all the same plane.

Answer: C
Explanation: A unique solution (x, y, z) corresponds to a single point where all three planes intersect.

Q530. A matrix is in row-echelon form. What can you do to solve the corresponding system of equations?
A) Perform Cramer's Rule
B) Find the inverse
C) Use back-substitution
D) The solution is immediately obvious.

Answer: C
Explanation: Row-echelon form creates a "triangular" system. You can solve for the last variable from the last equation, then substitute that value back into the equation above it to solve for the next variable, and so on.

More Questions - Mixed Review Batch 3

Q531. (Ch 10) Eigenvalues are associated with what type of matrix?
A) Any matrix
B) Rectangular matrix
C) Column matrix
D) Square matrix

Answer: D
Explanation: The eigenvalue problem Av=λv requires that the matrix A maps a vector from a space back into the same space, which means it must be a square (n x n) matrix.

Q532. (Ch 11) The sum of the probabilities P(X=x) for a discrete random variable X over all possible values of x is:
A) 0
B) 1
C) E(X)
D) Dependent on the distribution

Answer: B
Explanation: This is a fundamental axiom of probability theory. The total probability of the sample space must be 1.

Q533. (Ch 12) Decreasing the significance level α (e.g., from 0.05 to 0.01) makes it...
A) easier to reject H₀ and increases the risk of a Type I error.
B) harder to reject H₀ and decreases the risk of a Type I error.
C) harder to reject H₀ and increases the risk of a Type II error.
D) Both B and C are correct.

Answer: D
Explanation: A smaller α means you require stronger evidence to reject H₀. This decreases the probability of a Type I error (rejecting a true H₀) but increases the probability of a Type II error (failing to reject a false H₀). Both B and C are consequences.

Q534. (Ch 1) What is Re(i(3-4i))?
A) 3
B) -4
C) 4
D) -3

Answer: C
Explanation: i(3-4i) = 3i - 4i² = 3i - 4(-1) = 4 + 3i. The real part is 4.

Q535. (Ch 2) The number of terms in the expansion of (x+y+z)³ is:
A) 4
B) 6
C) 10
D) 9

Answer: C
Explanation: This is a multinomial expansion. The number of terms for (x₁+...+x_k)ⁿ is (n+k-1)C(k-1). Here n=3, k=3. So (3+3-1)C(3-1) = ⁵C₂ = 10.

Q536. (Ch 3) If P(x) has a leading term 2x⁵, as x→-∞, P(x)→?
A) ∞
B) -∞
C) 0
D) 2

Answer: B
Explanation: The degree (5) is odd, so the ends go in opposite directions. The leading coefficient (2) is positive, so it rises to the right and must fall to the left. As x→-∞, P(x)→-∞.

Q537. (Ch 4) Use log properties to solve log₂(x) + log₂(4) = 5.
A) 8
B) 16
C) 4
D) 32

Answer: A
Explanation: log₂(4x) = 5. In exponential form: 4x = 2⁵ = 32. x = 32/4 = 8.

Q538. (Ch 5) Find lim (x→∞) (e^x / x²).
A) 0
B) 1
C) ∞
D) Does not exist

Answer: C
Explanation: Exponential functions grow much faster than polynomial functions. As x gets large, the numerator grows significantly faster than the denominator. This can also be shown with two applications of L'Hopital's rule: lim e^x/x² -> lim e^x/2x -> lim e^x/2 = ∞.

Q539. (Ch 6) Evaluate ∫ dx / (1+x²).
A) ln(1+x²) + C
B) 2x / (1+x²)² + C
C) tan⁻¹(x) + C
D) -1 / (1+x)² + C

Answer: C
Explanation: This is the standard integral form for the inverse tangent (arctangent) function.

Q540. (Ch 7) If a mass-spring system is described by x''+4x=0, what is its natural angular frequency ω?
A) 4
B) 16
C) 1/2
D) 2

Answer: D
Explanation: The standard form for SHM is x''+ω²x=0. By comparing the two equations, ω²=4, so ω=2 rad/s.

Q541. (Ch 8) Find the area of the triangle with vertices P(0,0,0), Q(1,1,0) and R(0,1,1).
A) √3 / 2
B) √3
C) 1/2
D) 1

Answer: A
Explanation: The area is half the magnitude of the cross product of the vectors forming two sides, PQ = <1,1,0> and PR = <0,1,1>. PQ×PR = i-j+k. The magnitude is √3. The area of the triangle is (1/2)|PQ×PR| = √3 / 2.

Q542. (Ch 9) A is a 2x2 matrix and A⁻¹ = A. What could det(A) be?
A) 1 or -1
B) 1 only
C) -1 only
D) 0

Answer: A
Explanation: A⁻¹ = A => AA = AA⁻¹ => A² = I.
Taking the determinant of both sides: det(A²) = det(I) => (det(A))² = 1. Therefore, det(A) = ±1. An example is the identity matrix I or [[0,1],[1,0]].

Q543. (Ch 10) The set of all solutions to the homogeneous linear DE y'' - y = 0 forms a vector space. What is its dimension?
A) 0
B) 1
C) 2
D) Infinite

Answer: C
Explanation: The characteristic equation is m²-1=0, with roots m=±1. The general solution is y=C₁e^x + C₂e⁻ˣ. The solutions are all linear combinations of the two basis functions {e^x, e⁻ˣ}. Therefore, the solution space has a dimension of 2.

Q544. (Ch 11) The probability of event A is 0.6. The probability of event B is 0.5. If P(A∩B)=0.2, are the events independent?
A) Yes, because P(A)+P(B) > 1.
B) Yes, because P(A∩B) = P(A)P(B).
C) No, because P(A)+P(B) ≠ 1.
D) No, because P(A∩B) ≠ P(A)P(B).

Answer: D
Explanation: Two events are independent if P(A∩B) = P(A)P(B). Here, P(A)P(B) = 0.6 * 0.5 = 0.3. Since 0.2 ≠ 0.3, the events are not independent (they are dependent).

Q545. (Ch 12) In order to use a one-sample z-test for a population mean, which of the following is required?
A) The population standard deviation σ must be known.
B) The sample must be small (n<30).
C) The data must be categorical.
D) The population must be exactly normal.

Answer: A
Explanation: The z-test requires knowledge of the population standard deviation σ to calculate the standard error σ/√n. If σ is unknown, a t-test is used instead (which estimates σ with the sample standard deviation s). The CLT can relax the normality assumption for large samples, but the need for σ remains for the z-test.

Set 4 Questions - By Chapter
Chapter 1: Complex Numbers

Q546. The polar representation of a complex number is unique.
A) True
B) False

Answer: B
Explanation: The modulus r is unique, but the argument φ is not. You can add any multiple of 2π to φ and get the same complex number. A unique principal argument is usually defined to lie in an interval like (-π, π].

Q547. Evaluate Re(1/i).
A) 1
B) -1
C) i
D) 0

Answer: D
Explanation: 1/i = i/(i²) = i/(-1) = -i. This is 0 - 1i. The real part is 0.

Q548. Let z = -1 - i√3. What is |z|?
A) √2
B) 2
C) 4
D) 1+√3

Answer: B
Explanation: |z| = √((-1)² + (-√3)²) = √(1+3) = √4 = 2.

Q549. Using the previous question, what is the principal argument of z = -1 - i√3?
A) -π/3
B) 2π/3
C) 4π/3
D) -2π/3

Answer: D
Explanation: The point is in Q3. The reference angle is arctan(|-√3/-1|) = arctan(√3) = π/3. For Q3, the principal argument is φ = ref - π = π/3 - π = -2π/3.

Q550. If (a+bi)² = 8 + 6i, what is a² - b²?
A) 8
B) 6
C) 14
D) 10

Answer: A
Explanation: (a+bi)² = (a²-b²) + (2ab)i. We are given this equals 8 + 6i. Equating the real parts gives a² - b² = 8.

Chapter 2: Binomial Theorem and Series Expansions

Q551. Find the middle term in the expansion of (x + 1/x)¹⁰.
A) 252
B) 210 x²
C) 210
D) 252 x

Answer: A
Explanation: The expansion has 11 terms, so the middle term is the 6th term (r=5).
T₆ = ¹⁰C₅ (x)¹⁰⁻⁵ (1/x)⁵ = ¹⁰C₅ x⁵ x⁻⁵ = ¹⁰C₅ x⁰ = ¹⁰C₅ = (109876)/(54321) = 252.

Q552. The binomial expansion of (1+x)⁻¹/² represents:
A) ln(1+x)
B) 1/√(1+x)
C) e^x
D) sin(x)

Answer: B
Explanation: A negative power means reciprocal, and a power of 1/2 means square root. So (1+x)⁻¹/² is 1/√(1+x).

Q5alue of ³P₃?
A) 1
B) 3
C) 6
D) 9

Answer: C
Explanation: ³P₃ = 3! / (3-3)! = 3!/0! = 6/1 = 6. (The number of ways to arrange 3 items).

Q554. For the expansion of (1+3x)⁻¹, what is the condition of validity?
A) |x| < 3
B) |x| < 1
C) |x| < 1/3
D) All x

Answer: C
Explanation: Let y=3x. The expansion is valid for |y|<1 => |3x|<1 => 3|x|<1 => |x|<1/3.

Chapter 3: Polynomials and Rational Functions

Q555. What is the sum of the roots taken two at a time for the cubic ax³+bx²+cx+d=0?
A) c/a
B) -c/a
C) d/a
D) b/a

Answer: A
Explanation: This is one of Vieta's formulas. The sum of the roots is -b/a, the sum of roots taken two at a time (r₁r₂+r₁r₃+r₂r₃) is c/a, and the product of roots is -d/a.

Q556. The polynomial P(x)=x³-3x²+3x-1 is equal to:
A) (x+1)³
B) x³ - 1
C) (x-1)³
D) (x-1)(x²+1)

Answer: C
Explanation: This matches the pattern of the binomial expansion for (x-1)³ = x³ - 3x²(1) + 3x(1)² - 1³ = x³-3x²+3x-1.

Q557. Which of the following is true about the function f(x) = (x²+1)/(x²+2)?
A) It has a vertical asymptote.
B) It has a horizontal asymptote at y=0.
C) It has a horizontal asymptote at y=1.
D) It has a slant asymptote.

Answer: C
Explanation: The denominator x²+2 is never zero, so there are no vertical asymptotes. The degrees of the numerator and denominator are equal (2), so the HA is the ratio of leading coefficients: y=1/1 = 1.

Q558. A polynomial P(x) has a double root at x=1. This means which of the following is a factor of P(x)?
A) x-1
B) x+1
C) (x-1)²
D) (x+1)²

Answer: C
Explanation: A double root (multiplicity 2) at x=c means that (x-c)² is a factor of the polynomial.

Chapter 4: Logarithmic and Exponential Functions (Continued III)

Q559. If 10^x = y, then...
A) log_y(10) = x
B) log(y) = x
C) log(x) = y
D) ln(y) = x

Answer: B
Explanation: The common logarithm, log, has a base of 10. The statement 10^x = y in logarithmic form is log₁₀(y) = x, or simply log(y) = x.

Q560. A bacterial culture starts with 500 cells and doubles every hour. The model for the population P after t hours is:
A) P(t) = 500e^(2t)
B) P(t) = 500(2^t)
C) P(t) = 500 + 2t
D) P(t) = 500(t²)

Answer: B
Explanation: The initial amount is 500. Doubling means the growth factor is 2. The formula is P(t) = Initial * (Growth Factor)^t, so P(t) = 500(2^t).

Q561. Solve for t: 50 = 100e^(-0.1t).
A) t = 10 ln(2)
B) t = -10 ln(2)
C) t = 2 ln(10)
D) t = 10

Answer: A
Explanation: 50/100 = e^(-0.1t) => 0.5 = e^(-0.1t). Take natural log: ln(0.5) = -0.1t. Since ln(0.5)=ln(1/2)=-ln(2), we have -ln(2)=-0.1t => ln(2)=0.1t => t=ln(2)/0.1 = 10ln(2).

Q562. Simplify e^(ln(x²)).
A) x
B) x²
C) 2x
D) 2ln(x)

Answer: B
Explanation: The exponential function e^x and the natural logarithm ln(x) are inverse functions. Therefore, e^(ln(y)) = y. Here, y=x², so e^(ln(x²))=x².

Chapter 5: Limits, Continuity, and Differentiation (Continued IV)

Q563. Which theorem is used to prove lim (x→0) [x sin(1/x)] = 0?
A) Intermediate Value Theorem
B) Mean Value Theorem
C) Squeeze (or Sandwich) Theorem
D) L'Hôpital's Rule

Answer: C
Explanation: L'Hôpital's rule is not applicable here. We know -1 ≤ sin(1/x) ≤ 1. Multiplying by |x| gives -|x| ≤ x sin(1/x) ≤ |x|. As x→0, both -|x| and |x| approach 0. By the Squeeze Theorem, the function in the middle must also approach 0.

Q564. Find the equation of the tangent line to y=1/x at x=2.
A) y - 1/2 = (-1/4)(x-2)
B) y - 1/2 = (-1/2)(x-2)
C) y - 2 = (-1/4)(x-1/2)
D) y - 1/2 = (1/4)(x-2)

Answer: A
Explanation: y = x⁻¹. y' = -x⁻² = -1/x². At x=2, the slope is m = -1/2² = -1/4. The point is (2, 1/2). The equation is y - 1/2 = (-1/4)(x-2).

Q565. The acceleration of a particle is the rate of change of:
A) Position
B) Velocity
C) Displacement
D) Jerk

Answer: B
Explanation: Acceleration is the first derivative of velocity with respect to time (a = dv/dt) and the second derivative of position.

Q566. If a function is continuous at x=c, it must also be differentiable at x=c.
A) True
B) False

Answer: B
Explanation: Differentiability implies continuity, but the reverse is not true. A function can be continuous but not differentiable, for example, at a sharp corner like y=|x| at x=0.

Q567. Find the derivative of y = (sin x) / (cos x).
A) 1
B) -1
C) tan²(x)
D) sec²(x)

Answer: D
Explanation: y=tan(x). The derivative of tan(x) is sec²(x). Alternatively, using the quotient rule gives [cos(x)cos(x)-sin(x)(-sin(x))]/cos²x = [cos²x+sin²x]/cos²x = 1/cos²x = sec²x.

Chapter 6: Integration (Continued IV)

Q568. Which of the following is an improper integral?
A) ∫[0,1] x dx
B) ∫[1,∞] 1/x² dx
C) ∫[-1,1] x² dx
D) ∫[π,2π] sin(x) dx

Answer: B
Explanation: An improper integral is one where at least one of the limits of integration is infinite, or where the integrand is undefined at some point within the interval of integration.

Q5g9. The average value of a continuous function f(x) on [a,b] is the value c such that:
A) f(c) * (b-a) = ∫[a,b] f(x) dx
B) f'(c) * (b-a) = ∫[a,b] f(x) dx
C) c * (b-a) = ∫[a,b] f(x) dx
D) ∫[a,c] f(x)dx = ∫[c,b] f(x)dx

Answer: A
Explanation: The average value, f_avg, is defined as (1/(b-a)) ∫[a,b]f(x)dx. The Mean Value Theorem for Integrals guarantees that for a continuous function, there exists a 'c' in the interval such that f(c) = f_avg. Rearranging the formula gives this answer.

Q570. Find the volume when the region under y=1/x from x=1 to x=3 is rotated about the x-axis.
A) π ln(3)
B) 2π/3
C) π/3
D) 8π/9

Answer: B
Explanation: Volume = ∫[1,3] π y² dx = ∫[1,3] π(1/x)² dx = π∫[1,3] x⁻² dx.
V = π [-x⁻¹][1,3] = π [-1/x][1,3] = π [(-1/3) - (-1/1)] = π [-1/3 + 1] = π(2/3) = 2π/3.

Q571. To integrate ∫ (2x+3)/(x²+x) dx by partial fractions, the first step is:
A) Decompose it into A/x² + B/x
B) Factor the denominator as x(x+1)
C) Perform long division
D) Use u-substitution

Answer: B
Explanation: Before you can set up the partial fraction decomposition, you must fully factor the denominator. x²+x factors into x(x+1).

Chapter 7: Ordinary Differential Equations (Continued IV)

Q572. The general solution y=C₁cos(x)+C₂sin(x) represents what physical phenomenon?
A) Exponential decay
B) Unrestricted growth
C) Damped oscillation
D) Simple harmonic motion

Answer: D
Explanation: This sinusoidal solution is the hallmark of simple harmonic motion, which is undamped and oscillates forever. It comes from the DE y''+y=0.

Q573. A particular solution of a differential equation is one that is:
A) Very simple
B) Correct for all initial conditions
C) Free of any arbitrary constants
D) Found using partial fractions

Answer: C
Explanation: A particular solution is derived from the general solution by applying specific initial or boundary conditions to solve for the arbitrary constants C₁, C₂, etc.

Q574. If the integrating factor for y'+P(x)y=Q(x) is x³, what is P(x)?
A) 3/x
B) x³
C) 3x²
D) 3

Answer: A
Explanation: IF = e^(∫P(x)dx) = x³. Take the natural log of both sides: ∫P(x)dx = ln(x³) = 3ln(x). To find P(x), differentiate both sides with respect to x: P(x) = d/dx(3lnx) = 3/x.

Q575. For which of these DEs is y=e^(-2x) a solution?
A) y'' + 4y = 0
B) y' - 2y = 0
C) y' + 2y = 0
D) y'' + 4y' + 3y = 0

Answer: C
Explanation: If y=e⁻²ˣ, then y'=-2e⁻²ˣ.
y' + 2y = (-2e⁻²ˣ) + 2(e⁻²ˣ) = 0. So it is a solution to y'+2y=0.

Chapter 8: Vectors in Two and Three Dimensions (Continued IV)

Q576. The vectors u=<2,1> and v=<x,3> are parallel. Find x.
A) 3
B) 6
C) 1.5
D) -6

Answer: B
Explanation: If the vectors are parallel, their components are proportional. x/2 = 3/1. Therefore, x=6.

Q577. The distance between the points P(1,2,3) and Q(3,4,4) is:
A) 3
B) 9
C) √29
D) 29

Answer: A
Explanation: Distance = |PQ| = |Q-P| = |<3-1, 4-2, 4-3>| = |<2,2,1>|.
Magnitude = √(2²+2²+1²) = √(4+4+1) = √9 = 3.

Q578. The zero vector, 0⃗ = <0,0,0>, is:
A) Orthogonal to all vectors.
B) Parallel to all vectors.
C) Neither orthogonal nor parallel to any vector.
D) Both orthogonal and parallel to all vectors.

Answer: A
Explanation: The dot product of the zero vector with any other vector u is 0. u · 0⃗ = 0. Since the dot product is zero, by definition, the vectors are orthogonal. (The concept of parallel is based on non-zero scalar multiples, making it ambiguous, but orthogonality is clear from the dot product).

Q579. If two lines in 3D are not parallel and do not intersect, they are called:
A) Orthogonal
B) Skew
C) Collinear
D) Planar

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of skew lines. They exist in different non-parallel planes.

Q580. What does the dot product measure?
A) The tendency of one vector to rotate another.
B) The degree to which two vectors point in the same direction.
C) A vector that is perpendicular to two others.
D) The area of a parallelogram.

Answer: B
Explanation: The formula u·v=|u||v|cosθ shows the dot product is maximized when cosθ=1 (θ=0, vectors point in the same direction) and is zero when they are perpendicular. It is essentially the product of one vector's magnitude with the scalar projection of the other onto it.

Chapter 9: Matrices and Determinants (Continued III)

Q581. For matrices A and B, if A+B is defined, which is always true?
A) AB is defined.
B) BA is defined.
C) A^T B is defined.
D) A-B is defined.

Answer: D
Explanation: A+B being defined means A and B have the same order (m x n). If this is true, then A-B is also defined. The products AB, BA etc are not guaranteed to be defined unless the matrices are also square.

Q582. A 3x3 matrix has three identical rows. What is its determinant?
A) 3
B) 1
C) 0
D) Cannot be determined

Answer: C
Explanation: A property of determinants is that if any row (or column) is a multiple of another row (or column), the determinant is zero. Here, the rows are identical (a multiple of 1), so the determinant must be 0.

Q583. What is the identity element for matrix addition?
A) The identity matrix, I
B) The zero matrix, O
C) The number 1
D) The number 0

Answer: B
Explanation: The identity for an operation is the element that leaves another element unchanged. For matrix addition, A + O = A, so the zero matrix O is the additive identity.

Q584. If a matrix A has an inverse A⁻¹, then the solution to AX = 0 is:
A) X = A⁻¹
B) X = I
C) X = 0 (the trivial solution) only.
D) X can be non-zero.

Answer: C
Explanation: If A is invertible, we can multiply AX=0 by A⁻¹. A⁻¹AX = A⁻¹0 => IX = 0 => X=0. An invertible matrix guarantees that the only solution to the homogeneous system is the trivial one.

Q585. To use Cramer's rule for a 3x3 system AX=B, how many determinants must be calculated?
A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 4

Answer: D
Explanation: You must calculate the determinant of the main coefficient matrix (D), and then the determinants for each variable (D_x, D_y, D_z), where each one has a column replaced by the constant vector B. This is a total of 4 determinants.

More Questions - Mixed Review Batch 4

Q586. (Ch 10) Any spanning set for R³ must contain at least how many vectors?
A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 4

Answer: C
Explanation: The dimension of R³ is 3. To span a space of dimension n, you need at least n vectors.

Q587. (Ch 11) A bag contains 4 red and 6 blue balls. Two balls are drawn without replacement. What is the probability both are red?
A) 16/100
B) 12/100
C) 12/90
D) 20/90

Answer: C
Explanation: P(1st is Red) = 4/10. Given the first was red, P(2nd is Red) = 3/9. The probability of both is P(R₁) * P(R₂|R₁) = (4/10) * (3/9) = 12/90.

Q588. (Ch 12) A statistical test is conducted, and the result is "statistically significant at the 5% level". This means:
A) The p-value was greater than 0.05.
B) The null hypothesis was accepted.
C) The p-value was less than or equal to 0.05.
D) The study was important.

Answer: C
Explanation: "Statistically significant" means that the result was unlikely to have occurred by random chance alone, under the assumption of the null hypothesis. The threshold for this unlikeliness is the significance level α. So, significance at the 5% level means p ≤ 0.05.

Q589. (Ch 1) What is i⁻¹?
A) i
B) -1
C) 1
D) -i

Answer: D
Explanation: i⁻¹ = 1/i = i/(i²) = i/(-1) = -i.

Q590. (Ch 2) The approximation (1+x)ⁿ ≈ 1+nx is most accurate when:
A) n is large
B) n is an integer
C) x is very small
D) x is close to 1

Answer: C
Explanation: This is a first-order Taylor/binomial approximation. It works by ignoring higher powers of x (x², x³, etc.). This is a valid simplification only when x is very small, causing the higher power terms to be negligible.

Q591. (Ch 3) If P(x) divided by (x-5) gives a quotient of x+1 and a remainder of 3, what is P(x)?
A) (x-5)(x+1) + 3
B) (x-5)(x+1) - 3
C) (x+1)(x-5) * 3
D) (x-5) / (x+1) + 3

Answer: A
Explanation: The division algorithm states P(x) = D(x)Q(x) + R(x). So, P(x) = (x-5)(x+1) + 3.

Q592. (Ch 4) Solve logₓ(64) = 2.
A) 32
B) 128
C) 8
D) 4

Answer: C
Explanation: Rewrite in exponential form: x² = 64. Since the base x must be positive, x=8.

Q593. (Ch 5) Find lim (x→0) (1-cos(x)) / x.
A) 1
B) 0
C) -1
D) ∞

Answer: B
Explanation: Using L'Hôpital's Rule (since it's 0/0 form): lim (d/dx(1-cosx)) / (d/dx(x)) = lim (sin x) / 1 = sin(0) = 0.

Q594. (Ch 6) The integral ∫ x e^(x²) dx evaluates to:
A) (1/2) e^(x²) + C
B) x e^(x²) + C
C) 2x e^(x²) + C
D) e^(x²) + C

Answer: A
Explanation: Use u-sub with u=x². du=2xdx => xdx = du/2. ∫ e^u (du/2) = (1/2)∫e^u du = (1/2)e^u + C = (1/2)e^(x²) + C.

Q595. (Ch 7) Find a solution to y'' - y' = 0.
A) y = sin(x)
B) y = x²
C) y = e^x
D) y = x

Answer: C
Explanation: Characteristic eq: m²-m=0 => m(m-1)=0. Roots m=0, m=1. General solution is y=C₁e^(0x) + C₂e^(1x) = C₁ + C₂e^x. A specific solution would be when C₁=0 and C₂=1, giving y=e^x. (y=x is not a solution: 0-1≠0).

Q596. (Ch 8) For any two vectors u and v, |u+v| ≤ |u|+|v|. This is known as the:
A) Pythagorean Theorem
B) Law of Cosines
C) Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality
D) Triangle Inequality

Answer: D
Explanation: This is the Triangle Inequality for vectors. It states that the length of one side of a triangle (formed by vectors u, v, and u+v) cannot be greater than the sum of the lengths of the other two sides.

Q597. (Ch 9) A matrix A is an upper triangular matrix if:
A) a_ij = 0 for i=j
B) a_ij = 0 for i>j
C) a_ij = 0 for i<j
D) a_ij = 0 for i≠j

Answer: B
Explanation: An upper triangular matrix has all zero elements below the main diagonal. The position of an element is below the diagonal if its row number (i) is greater than its column number (j).

Q598. (Ch 10) The null space of a matrix A is the set of all vectors x such that:
A) Ax = I
B) Ax = x
C) Ax = A⁻¹
D) Ax = 0

Answer: D
Explanation: The null space (or kernel) of a matrix/linear transformation is the set of all vectors that are mapped to the zero vector. It is always a subspace.

Q599. (Ch 11) In a lottery, 100 tickets are sold and there is one winner. If you buy 5 tickets, what is your probability of winning?
A) 0.01
B) 0.95
C) 0.05
D) 0.1

Answer: C
Explanation: You have 5 favorable outcomes out of a total of 100 possible outcomes. P(win) = 5/100 = 0.05.

Q600. (Ch 12) In a right-tailed hypothesis test, the p-value is the area...
A) To the left of the test statistic.
B) To the right of the test statistic.
C) On both tails of the distribution.
D) Between the test statistic and the mean.

Answer: B
Explanation: In a right-tailed test (H₁: μ > μ₀), the p-value represents the probability of getting a result as large as or larger than the observed test statistic. This corresponds to the area in the right tail of the sampling distribution.

Set 5 Questions - By Chapter
Chapter 1: Complex Numbers

Q601. De Moivre's theorem is used for finding:
A) Sums of complex numbers
B) Arguments of complex numbers
C) Powers and roots of complex numbers
D) Inverses of complex numbers

Answer: C
Explanation: The theorem [r(cosφ+isinφ)]ⁿ = rⁿ(cos(nφ)+isin(nφ)) is a powerful tool for calculating integer powers and, when rearranged, the n-th roots of complex numbers.

Q602. What is the complex number z if |z|=2 and arg(z)=π?
A) 2
B) -2
C) 2i
D) -2i

Answer: B
Explanation: The point is on the negative real axis at a distance of 2 from the origin. This corresponds to the complex number -2 + 0i.

Q603. Find (3+i)/(3-i) in Cartesian form.
A) 4/5 + 3/5 i
B) 1
C) (4+3i)/4
D) 3/4 + 4/5 i

Answer: A
Explanation: [(3+i)(3+i)]/[(3-i)(3+i)] = [9+6i+i²]/[3²+1²] = [9+6i-1]/10 = [8+6i]/10 = 8/10 + 6i/10 = 4/5 + 3/5 i.

Q604. If z=x+iy, then Im(z²) is:
A) y²
B) x²-y²
C) 2xy
D) x²+y²

Answer: C
Explanation: z² = (x+iy)² = x² + 2(x)(iy) + (iy)² = x² + 2xyi - y² = (x²-y²) + (2xy)i. The imaginary part is 2xy.

Q605. Find the sum of the roots of the equation z⁴ = 1.
A) 0
B) 1
C) 4
D) 2π

Answer: A
Explanation: The four 4th roots of unity (1, i, -1, -i) are equally spaced on the unit circle. Their sum is 1 + i - 1 - i = 0. The sum of the n-th roots of unity is always 0 for n>1.

Chapter 2: Binomial Theorem and Series Expansions

Q606. For which value of n is ¹⁰C_n the greatest?
A) n=1
B) n=10
C) n=5
D) n=9

Answer: C
Explanation: For an even power k, kCr is maximum at r=k/2. Here k=10, so ¹⁰C₅ is the largest binomial coefficient.

Q607. Which series represents an application of the Generalized Binomial Theorem?
A) The Taylor series for sin(x)
B) The geometric series 1+r+r²+...
C) A Fourier series
D) The sum of an arithmetic progression

Answer: B
Explanation: The geometric series is the expansion of (1-r)⁻¹, which is a case of the generalized binomial theorem where n=-1 and x=-r.

Q608. Find the value of n if the coefficient of x in (1+x)ⁿ is 7.
A) 1
B) 7
C) 6
D) 8

Answer: B
Explanation: The x term is nC₁ x¹ = nx. The coefficient is n. If nx = 7x, then n=7.

Q609. The number 5! represents:
A) The sum of the first 5 integers.
B) The number of ways to choose 1 item from 5.
C) The number of ways to arrange 5 distinct items.
D) The 5th prime number.

Answer: C
Explanation: n-factorial (n!) represents the number of permutations, or arrangements in order, of n distinct objects.

Chapter 3: Polynomials and Rational Functions

Q610. If the roots of a quadratic equation are α and β, the equation can be written as:
A) x² + (α+β)x + αβ = 0
B) x² - (α+β)x - αβ = 0
C) x² - (α+β)x + αβ = 0
D) x² + (αβ)x - (α+β) = 0

Answer: C
Explanation: The equation is (x-α)(x-β) = x² - βx - αx + αβ = x² - (α+β)x + αβ = 0. Sum of roots is -b/a, product is c/a. So b=-(α+β), c=αβ (for a=1).

Q611. The range of a polynomial function with an odd degree is:
A) All positive real numbers.
B) All negative real numbers.
C) All real numbers.
D) A finite interval.

Answer: C
Explanation: A polynomial with an odd degree has end behavior where one end goes to +∞ and the other to -∞. Since the graph is continuous, it must pass through all real y-values in between. Its range is (-∞, ∞).

Q612. The graph of y=1/(x-2)² has vertical asymptote x=2. What is the behavior of y as x approaches 2?
A) y → ∞ from both sides.
B) y → -∞ from both sides.
C) y → ∞ from the right and y → -∞ from the left.
D) The limit does not exist.

Answer: A
Explanation: Since the denominator is squared, (x-2)² is always positive, whether x approaches 2 from the left or right. A positive number divided by a very small positive number approaches +∞.

More Questions - Mixed Review Batch 5

Q613. (Ch 4) What is the value of e^(2 ln 3)?
A) 6
B) 9
C) 8
D) ln(9)

Answer: B
Explanation: Using log properties, 2 ln 3 = ln(3²)=ln(9). Then e^(ln 9) = 9, because e^x and ln(x) are inverse functions.

Q614. (Ch 5) If f'(c) does not exist, can x=c be a critical point?
A) Yes, if f(c) exists.
B) No, the derivative must be zero.
C) Only if f''(c) = 0.
D) Only for polynomial functions.

Answer: A
Explanation: Yes. A critical point is a point where f'(c)=0 OR f'(c) is undefined. For example, f(x)=³√x has f'(0) undefined, so x=0 is a critical point.

Q615. (Ch 6) The technique for reversing the chain rule in integration is:
A) Integration by parts
B) u-Substitution
C) Partial Fractions
D) The power rule

Answer: B
Explanation: u-Substitution is the direct counterpart to the chain rule for differentiation. It is used to integrate composite functions.

Q616. (Ch 7) Laplace's Equation (∇²u=0) is primarily used to describe:
A) Wave propagation
B) Heat diffusion over time
C) Steady-state phenomena
D) Damped oscillations

Answer: C
Explanation: Laplace's Equation describes systems that have reached a state of equilibrium, where there is no change over time (e.g., steady-state temperature distribution, electrostatic potential in a charge-free region).

Q617. (Ch 8) Find the midpoint of the line segment connecting P(1,-2,5) and Q(3,4,-1).
A) (2,1,2)
B) (2,2,4)
C) (4,2,4)
D) (1,3,-3)

Answer: A
Explanation: The midpoint is the average of the coordinates: ((1+3)/2, (-2+4)/2, (5-1)/2) = (4/2, 2/2, 4/2) = (2,1,2).

Q618. (Ch 9) Let A be a 2x3 matrix. The transpose A^T will have an order of:
A) 2x3
B) 3x2
C) 2x2
D) 3x3

Answer: B
Explanation: The transpose operation swaps the number of rows and columns. A 2x3 matrix becomes a 3x2 matrix.

Q619. (Ch 10) If a set of vectors S spans a vector space V, and T is a set containing S (T ⊃ S), then:
A) T also spans V.
B) T is a basis for V.
C) T is linearly independent.
D) T does not span V.

Answer: A
Explanation: If a set of vectors can already create every vector in the space, then adding more vectors to that set won't take away that ability. The new, larger set will still span the space (though it may now be linearly dependent).

Q620. (Ch 11) Which of the following is NOT a discrete random variable?
A) The number of heads in 10 coin flips.
B) The number of cars that pass a point in an hour.
C) The height of a randomly selected student.
D) The score on a standard 6-sided die roll.

Answer: C
Explanation: Height is a continuous variable because it can take on any value within a range (e.g., 175.1 cm, 175.11 cm, etc.). The other options are discrete as they can only take on integer values.

Q621. (Ch 12) A survey of 1000 people finds 300 support a policy. A 95% confidence interval for the true proportion is calculated. What is the sample proportion p̂?
A) 3
B) 0.03
C) 30
D) 0.3

Answer: D
Explanation: The sample proportion is p̂ = x/n = 300/1000 = 0.3.

Q622. (Ch 1) Find i + i³.
A) 2i
B) 0
C) -2i
D) -1

Answer: B
Explanation: i³ = -i. So i + i³ = i + (-i) = 0.

Q623. (Ch 5) lim (x→∞) sin(x)
A) 0
B) 1
C) ∞
D) Does not exist

Answer: D
Explanation: As x approaches infinity, the sin(x) function continues to oscillate between -1 and 1. It does not settle on a single value, so the limit does not exist.

Q624. (Ch 9) Let A = [[cosθ, -sinθ],[sinθ, cosθ]]. Find det(A).
A) 1
B) cos(2θ)
C) sin(2θ)
D) 0

Answer: A
Explanation: This is a rotation matrix. det(A) = (cosθ)(cosθ) - (-sinθ)(sinθ) = cos²θ + sin²θ = 1.

Q625. (Ch 11) For any event A, what is P(A|A)?
A) 0
B) P(A)
C) P(A)²
D) 1

Answer: D
Explanation: Given that event A has already occurred, the probability that event A occurs is 1 (or 100%). P(A|A) = P(A∩A)/P(A) = P(A)/P(A) = 1.

Set 6 Questions - By Chapter
Chapter 1: Complex Numbers

Q626. Find (√2cis(π/4))²
A) 2i
B) -2i
C) 2
D) 4i

Answer: A
Explanation: Using De Moivre's theorem: (√2)² cis(2*π/4) = 2 cis(π/2) = 2(cos(π/2)+isin(π/2)) = 2(0+i) = 2i.

Q627. If z₁=r₁cis(θ₁) and z₂=r₂cis(θ₂), then |z₁+z₂|=?
A) r₁ + r₂
B) √(r₁²+r₂²)
C) r₁r₂
D) √(r₁²+r₂² + 2r₁r₂cos(θ₁-θ₂))

Answer: D
Explanation: This is the Law of Cosines applied to the triangle formed by the vectors z₁, z₂, and their sum z₁+z₂ in the Argand diagram. There is no simple rule for the modulus of a sum like there is for a product.

Q628. Find Im(z) if z = 5 / (2+i).
A) -1
B) 1
C) 2
D) -2

Answer: A
Explanation: z = [5(2-i)]/[(2+i)(2-i)] = [10-5i]/[4+1] = [10-5i]/5 = 2 - i. The imaginary part is -1.

Chapter 2: Binomial Theorem and Series Expansions

Q629. If n is a positive odd integer, the expansion of (x+y)ⁿ has:
A) One middle term
B) Two middle terms
C) No middle terms
D) n middle terms

Answer: B
Explanation: An odd power n results in n+1 (even) terms. An even number of terms has two middle terms. E.g., for n=3, there are 4 terms, and the 2nd and 3rd are the middle ones.

Q630. Find the first term in the expansion of (1+x²)⁻¹.
A) 1
B) -1
C) x²
D) -x²

Answer: A
Explanation: For any generalized binomial expansion of (1+y)ⁿ, the first term is always 1 (since 1ⁿ=1 and the coefficient is 1).

Q631. Find the term in x³ in the expansion of e^x.
A) x³/3
B) x³/2
C) x³/6
D) x³/24

Answer: C
Explanation: The Maclaurin series for e^x is 1 + x + x²/2! + x³/3! + ... The term is x³/3! = x³/6.

Chapter 3: Polynomials and Rational Functions

Q632. Given P(x)=x³-ax²+bx-6 has a factor (x-1) and leaves a remainder of -4 when divided by (x+1). Find a.
A) 3
B) -3
C) 2
D) -2

Answer: C
Explanation: (x-1) is a factor => P(1)=0 => 1-a+b-6=0 => -a+b=5 (Eq 1).
Remainder with (x+1) is -4 => P(-1)=-4 => -1-a-b-6=-4 => -a-b=3 (Eq 2).
Add (Eq 1) and (Eq 2): -2a = 8 => a = -4. Wait, check calc.
P(1)=1-a+b-6 = -a+b-5=0 -> -a+b=5
P(-1)=-1-a-b-6=-a-b-7=-4 -> -a-b=3
(-a+b)+(-a-b)=5+3 -> -2a=8 -> a=-4. I seem to have an issue.
Let's check P(x)=x³-2x²+x-6. (x-1) not a factor.
P(x)=x³-2x²+3x-6. Remainder -4 with x+1? -1-2-3-6=-12.
Let's retry solving the system of equations.
-a+b = 5
-a-b = 3
(1)+(2) => -2a = 8 => a=-4.
(1)-(2) => 2b = 2 => b=1.
Let's check. P(x) = x³+4x²+x-6. P(1)=1+4+1-6=0. Factor is good.
P(-1)=-1+4-1-6 = -4. Remainder is good. So a=-4. Where did I get a=2?
Maybe the initial options I generated were wrong. Let me regenerate one that makes sense.
Option A: a=3. Then -3+b=5,b=8. -3-b=3,-b=6,b=-6. No.
Option C: a=2. Then -2+b=5, b=7. -2-b=3,-b=5,b=-5. No.
The algebra is leading to a=-4, b=1. I will set the question to find 'b' instead.
Q632. (Revised) Given P(x)=x³-ax²+bx-6 has a factor (x-1) and leaves a remainder of -4 when divided by (x+1). Find b.
A) 1
B) -1
C) 5
D) -3

Answer: A
Explanation: From the reasoning above, solving the system {-a+b=5, -a-b=3} gives a=-4 and b=1.

Q633. The graph y = (x³+1)/(x+1) simplifies to...
A) y = x²-x+1
B) y = x²-x+1, with a hole at x=-1
C) y = x²
D) y = x²+1

Answer: B
Explanation: The numerator is a sum of cubes: x³+1 = (x+1)(x²-x+1). So f(x) = (x+1)(x²-x+1)/(x+1). The (x+1) terms cancel, leaving the parabola y=x²-x+1. However, the original function was undefined at x=-1, so there is a hole in the graph at that point.

Chapter 4: Logarithmic and Exponential Functions

Q634. Evaluate log(10√10).
A) 1
B) 2/3
C) 1.5
D) 2.5

Answer: C
Explanation: log(10 * 10^(1/2)) = log(10^(3/2)) = 3/2 = 1.5.

Q635. Which function grows fastest as x→∞?
A) y = x²
B) y = 100 log(x)
C) y = (1.1)^x
D) y = x¹⁰⁰

Answer: C
Explanation: Exponential functions (where the variable is in the exponent) always grow faster than any polynomial or logarithmic function for large x.

More Questions - Mixed Review Batch 6

Q636. (Ch 5) Which function is NOT continuous everywhere?
A) f(x) = sin(x)
B) f(x) = x³ - 2
C) f(x) = e^x
D) f(x) = tan(x)

Answer: D
Explanation: The tangent function, tan(x)=sin(x)/cos(x), has infinite discontinuities (vertical asymptotes) wherever cos(x)=0, such as at x = ±π/2, ±3π/2, etc.

Q637. (Ch 6) If f(x) is an even function (f(-x)=f(x)), then ∫[-a,a] f(x) dx = ?
A) 0
B) f(a) - f(-a)
C) 2 ∫[0,a] f(x) dx
D) 2a * f(a)

Answer: C
Explanation: Because of the symmetry about the y-axis, the area from -a to 0 is the same as the area from 0 to a. So the total integral is twice the integral over the positive half.

Q638. (Ch 7) What is the order and linearity of the DE: y' + sin(y) = x?
A) First order, linear
B) First order, non-linear
C) Second order, linear
D) Second order, non-linear

Answer: B
Explanation: The highest derivative is y', so it is first-order. However, the dependent variable y is inside the sin() function, which makes the equation non-linear.

Q639. (Ch 8) Find a vector of magnitude 10 in the direction of v = <3, 0, -4>.
A) <6, 0, -8>
B) <30, 0, -40>
C) <3, 0, -4>
D) <10, 0, -10>

Answer: A
Explanation: The magnitude of v is |v|=√(3²+0²+(-4)²)=√25=5. A unit vector in its direction is u = <3/5, 0, -4/5>. A vector of magnitude 10 in this direction is 10u = 10*<3/5, 0, -4/5> = <6, 0, -8>.

Q640. (Ch 9) For two n x n matrices A and B, which statement is FALSE?
A) det(A+B) = det(A)+det(B)
B) det(AB) = det(A)det(B)
C) det(A^T) = det(A)
D) det(I) = 1

Answer: A
Explanation: The determinant is generally NOT additive. All the other statements are true properties of determinants.

Q641. (Ch 10) Eigenvectors corresponding to distinct eigenvalues are...
A) Always orthogonal
B) Linearly dependent
C) Linearly independent
D) Always parallel

Answer: C
Explanation: This is a key theorem in linear algebra. If eigenvalues λ₁, λ₂, ..., λ_k are all different, then their corresponding eigenvectors v₁, v₂, ..., v_k form a linearly independent set.

Q642. (Ch 11) A coin is biased so that P(Heads)=0.6. If it's flipped twice, what's P(one Head, one Tail)?
A) 0.48
B) 0.24
C) 0.5
D) 0.36

Answer: A
Explanation: P(Tails)=0.4. The two ways to get one of each are HT and TH.
P(HT) = 0.6 * 0.4 = 0.24.
P(TH) = 0.4 * 0.6 = 0.24.
Since these are mutually exclusive, the total probability is 0.24 + 0.24 = 0.48.

Q643. (Ch 12) A random sample of students is taken to estimate the average study time at a university. This is an example of:
A) A census
B) Descriptive statistics
C) A designed experiment
D) An observational study (survey)

Answer: D
Explanation: This is an observational study, specifically a sample survey, where a sample is used to infer properties of the larger population without imposing any treatment. A census would involve all students.

Q644. (Ch 1) What is (z̅)̅ ?
A) z
B) z̅
C) -z
D) 1/z

Answer: A
Explanation: Taking the conjugate twice returns you to the original complex number. (a+bi) -> (a-bi) -> (a+bi).

Q645. (Ch 5) lim (h→0) [tan(x+h) - tan(x)] / h = ?
A) tan(x)
B) sec(x)
C) cot(x)
D) sec²(x)

Answer: D
Explanation: This is the limit definition of the derivative of the function f(x)=tan(x). The derivative of tan(x) is sec²(x).

Q646. (Ch 9) If A²=I for a square matrix A, then A is...
A) The identity matrix
B) The zero matrix
C) Its own inverse
D) Singular

Answer: C
Explanation: If A²=I, this means AA=I. By definition of the inverse (AA⁻¹=I), this means A⁻¹=A. The matrix is called an involutory matrix.

Q647. (Ch 11) The mean and variance of a certain Poisson distribution are equal to 5. What is the parameter λ?
A) 5
B) 25
C) √5
D) Cannot be determined

Answer: A
Explanation: For a Poisson distribution, both the mean and the variance are equal to the parameter λ. Therefore, λ=5.

Q648. (Ch 1) Simplify (4i)/(2i).
A) 2
B) 2i
C) 1/2
D) i

Answer: A
Explanation: The i terms cancel out, leaving 4/2 = 2.

Q649. (Ch 2) If the coefficient of x² in (1-3x)ⁿ is 135, what is n?
A) 5
B) 6
C) 9
D) 10

Answer: B
Explanation: The x² term is nC₂ (-3x)² = [n(n-1)/2] * 9x².
Coefficient is (9/2)n(n-1) = 135 => n(n-1) = 1352/9 = 30.
By inspection, 6(6-1)=30, so n=6.

Q650. (Ch 3) How many x-intercepts can the graph of a degree 5 polynomial have at most?
A) 4
B) 5
C) 6
D) 1

Answer: B
Explanation: The number of x-intercepts (real roots) of a polynomial of degree n is at most n.

Q651. (Ch 4) What is the value of x if log₃(log₂(x)) = 1?
A) 2
B) 3
C) 8
D) 9

Answer: C
Explanation: Work from the outside in. First, rewrite log₃(y)=1 as y=3¹. So log₂(x)=3. Then rewrite log₂(x)=3 as x=2³, which is x=8.

Q562. (Ch 8) Let u=i+j and v=j+k. The cosine of the angle between them is:
A) 0
B) 1
C) 1/2
D) 1/√2

Answer: C
Explanation: u=<1,1,0>, v=<0,1,1>. u·v = 1. |u|=√2, |v|=√2. cosθ = 1/(√2√2) = 1/2.

Q653. (Ch 12) As degrees of freedom increase, the t-distribution approaches the...
A) F-distribution
B) Chi-square distribution
C) Standard normal distribution
D) Binomial distribution

Answer: C
Explanation: The t-distribution has heavier tails than the standard normal distribution (Z), especially for small df. As the sample size (and thus df) gets larger, the t-distribution becomes virtually indistinguishable from the Z-distribution.

Q654. (Ch 1) What is z + z̅ if z=7-2i?
A) 14
B) -4i
C) 14-4i
D) 0

Answer: A
Explanation: z̅ = 7+2i. z+z̅ = (7-2i)+(7+2i) = 14. In general z+z̅=2Re(z).

Q655. (Ch 5) For what values of x is the function f(x) = (x-1)/(x-2) decreasing?
A) x > 2
B) x < 2
C) For all x in its domain.
D) For no values of x.

Answer: C
Explanation: Find f'(x) using the quotient rule: f'(x) = [1(x-2)-1(x-1)]/(x-2)² = [x-2-x+1]/(x-2)² = -1/(x-2)².
The denominator (x-2)² is always positive. The numerator is -1. Thus, f'(x) is always negative for all x where it is defined (x≠2). So the function is always decreasing on its domain.

Set 7 Questions - By Chapter
Chapter 6: Integration

Q656. Evaluate ∫ 1/(x-5) dx.
A) -1/(x-5)² + C
B) ln|x-5| + C
C) ln|x| - ln|5| + C
D) 1/5 ln|x| + C

Answer: B
Explanation: This is a direct application of the rule ∫ 1/u du = ln|u| + C, where u = x-5.

Q657. Which method would be best to evaluate ∫ x² sin(x) dx?
A) u-Substitution
B) Partial Fractions
C) Integration by Parts (twice)
D) Trigonometric Substitution

Answer: C
Explanation: This is a product of a polynomial and a trig function. Integration by parts is needed. Because the polynomial is x², it needs to be differentiated twice to disappear, so parts must be applied twice.

Q658. The area enclosed by the x-axis, the line y=2x, and the line x=3 is:
A) 9
B) 18
C) 6
D) 3

Answer: A
Explanation: The area is ∫[0,3] 2x dx = [x²]_[0,3] = 3² - 0² = 9. This is also the area of a triangle with base 3 and height y(3)=6. Area=(1/2)bh=(1/2)(3)(6)=9.

Q659. The integral ∫[0,4] √x dx is equal to:
A) 16/3
B) 8/3
C) 4
D) 2

Answer: A
Explanation: ∫x¹/²dx = (2/3)x³/². Evaluating: (2/3)[x³/²]_[0,4] = (2/3)[4³/² - 0] = (2/3)[(√4)³] = (2/3)[2³] = (2/3)(8)=16/3.

Chapter 7: Ordinary Differential Equations

Q660. The degree of the DE (y'')³ + y' + y = 0 is:
A) 1
B) 2
C) 3
D) 6

Answer: C
Explanation: The degree is the power of the highest-order derivative. The highest derivative is y'', and it is raised to the power of 3.

Q661. Which DE is NOT linear?
A) y'' + 2y' + y = x
B) y' + xy = 1
C) y' + y² = 0
D) y''' + 5y = 0

Answer: C
Explanation: The term y² makes the equation non-linear because the dependent variable y is not to the first power.

Q662. The solution to dT/dt = -k(T-T_a) shows that the temperature T of the object approaches...
A) Infinity
B) Zero
C) The initial temperature T₀
D) The ambient temperature T_a

Answer: D
Explanation: The solution is T(t) = T_a + (T₀-T_a)e^(-kt). As t→∞, the exponential term goes to zero, leaving T(t)→T_a. The object's temperature approaches the surrounding temperature.

Chapter 8: Vectors in Two and Three Dimensions

Q663. What is the standard notation for a position vector?
A) v
B) p
C) r
D) s

Answer: C
Explanation: By convention, the vector from the origin to a point (x,y,z) is called the position vector and is usually denoted by r.

Q664. If u·v=0 and u×v=0 for two vectors u and v, then:
A) u and v are parallel
B) u and v are orthogonal
C) At least one of u or v must be the zero vector.
D) This is impossible.

Answer: C
Explanation: u·v=0 implies they are orthogonal. u×v=0 implies they are parallel. A non-zero vector cannot be both orthogonal and parallel to another non-zero vector. Therefore, one or both of them must be the zero vector.

Q665. The diver-gience of a vector field (∇·F) results in a:
A) Vector Field
B) Scalar Field
C) Constant
D) Zero Vector

Answer: B
Explanation: The divergence operation, which involves taking partial derivatives of the vector components and summing them, results in a scalar field that measures the 'outflow' at a point.

More Questions - Mixed Review Batch 7

Q666. (Ch 9) Which matrix does not have an inverse?
A) [[1,1],[1,2]]
B) [[0,1],[1,0]]
C) [[1,2],[3,4]]
D) [[2,1],[4,2]]

Answer: D
Explanation: A matrix has no inverse if its determinant is zero. det([[2,1],[4,2]]) = 22 - 14 = 0.

Q667. (Ch 10) Which of the following is NOT a vector space (with standard operations)?
A) The set of all 2x2 matrices.
B) The set of all continuous functions on [0,1].
C) The set of all vectors <x,y> where x+y=1.
D) The set of all polynomials of degree ≤ 5.

Answer: C
Explanation: To be a subspace, it must contain the zero vector. The zero vector <0,0> does not satisfy x+y=1 (since 0+0≠1). Therefore it's not a vector space.

Q668. (Ch 11) A number is chosen from {1, 2, 3, 4, 5}. What is the probability it is even or prime?
A) 1
B) 4/5
C) 3/5
D) 2/5

Answer: B
Explanation: Evens: {2, 4}. Primes: {2, 3, 5}. E ∪ P = {2, 3, 4, 5}. There are 4 unique numbers in the union out of 5 total numbers. P(E∪P) = P(E)+P(P)-P(E∩P) = 2/5 + 3/5 - 1/5 = 4/5.

Q669. (Ch 12) Increasing the confidence level from 95% to 99% will cause the margin of error to:
A) Increase
B) Decrease
C) Stay the same
D) Double

Answer: A
Explanation: To be more confident, you need a wider interval. The critical value (Z* or t*) increases for a higher confidence level, which in turn increases the margin of error.

Q670. (Ch 1) What is arg(1-i)?
A) π/4
B) -π/4
C) 3π/4
D) 7π/4

Answer: B
Explanation: The point (1,-1) is in the 4th quadrant. The reference angle is arctan(|-1/1|) = π/4. For Q4, the principal argument is -ref = -π/4.

Q671. (Ch 5) Differentiate y = log₁₀(x).
A) 1/x
B) 10/x
C) 1/(x ln 10)
D) ln(10)/x

Answer: C
Explanation: Use the change of base formula first: y = ln(x)/ln(10). Since 1/ln(10) is a constant, the derivative is y' = (1/ln 10) * (1/x) = 1/(x ln 10).

Q672. (Ch 3) According to the Fundamental Theorem of Algebra, a polynomial of degree n has:
A) exactly n real roots.
B) at most n real roots.
C) exactly n complex roots (counting multiplicity).
D) at least one real root.

Answer: C
Explanation: The theorem guarantees exactly n roots in the complex number system, provided you count repeated roots according to their multiplicity.

Q673. (Ch 8) Find the magnitude of torque τ = r × F if r=<2,0,0>, F=<0,5,0>, and the angle between them is 90°.
A) 0
B) 7
C) 10
D) 10k

Answer: C
Explanation: Magnitude |τ| = |r||F|sinθ. |r|=2, |F|=5, θ=90°. |τ|=25sin(90°)=10*1=10. Alternatively, the vector is r×F = <2,0,0>×<0,5,0> = 10k. Its magnitude is 10.

Q674. (Ch 4) What is the horizontal asymptote of y = e^x?
A) y = 0
B) y = 1
C) x = 0
D) There is none.
Question needs to be more specific
Q674. (Revised) What is the horizontal asymptote of y=e^x as x approaches -∞?
A) y = 0
B) y = 1
C) y = ∞
D) y = -∞

Answer: A
Explanation: As x becomes a large negative number, e^x approaches 0 (e.g., e⁻¹⁰⁰ is very close to zero). Therefore, the line y=0 is a horizontal asymptote on the left side.

Q675. (Ch 1) What is the relationship between the two cube roots of 1 (other than 1 itself)?
A) They are additive inverses.
B) They are multiplicative inverses.
C) They are complex conjugates.
D) They are equal.

Answer: C
Explanation: The three cube roots of 1 are 1, ω = -1/2 + i√3/2, and ω² = -1/2 - i√3/2. The two non-real roots are complex conjugates of each other.

Q676. (Ch 2) If the terms in the expansion of (x+y)ⁿ are 1, 6, 15, ..., what is n?
A) 3
B) 5
C) 6
D) 15

Answer: C
Explanation: The coefficients are the entries of Pascal's triangle. The row that starts 1, 6, 15... corresponds to the power n=6. These are ⁶C₀, ⁶C₁, ⁶C₂.

Q677. (Ch 6) If f(x) ≥ 0 on [a,b], then ∫[a,b] f(x) dx represents:
A) Net area
B) Signed area
C) Geometric area
D) The function's average value

Answer: C
Explanation: When the function is entirely non-negative, there is no area below the axis to subtract. Therefore, the definite integral (net signed area) is the same as the total geometric area under the curve.

Q678. (Ch 7) What is a crucial first step in solving a first-order linear DE y'+P(x)y=Q(x)?
A) Ensure it is separable.
B) Find the characteristic equation.
C) Identify P(x) and Q(x) correctly.
D) Differentiate the equation again.

Answer: C
Explanation: To use the integrating factor method, you must have the equation in standard form so that you can correctly identify P(x), which is needed to calculate the integrating factor.

Q679. (Ch 9) Which operation is defined for any two matrices A and B of the same order, m x n?
A) AB
B) A⁻¹
C) A+B
D) det(A)

Answer: C
Explanation: Addition (and subtraction) is defined for any two matrices as long as their dimensions are identical. Product (AB) is only defined if n=m. Inverse and determinant are only defined for square matrices (n=m).

Q680. (Ch 10) Can a single vector {v} be a linearly dependent set?
A) Yes, if v is the zero vector.
B) Yes, if v is a unit vector.
C) No, a single vector is always linearly independent.
D) No, you need at least two vectors.

Answer: A
Explanation: A set is linearly dependent if there's a non-trivial solution to c₁v₁ +...=0. For the set {v}, we look at cv=0. If v=0⃗, then we can choose any non-zero c (like c=1), and 1*0⃗=0⃗. Since a non-trivial solution for c exists, the set {0⃗} is linearly dependent.

Q681. (Ch 12) In the context of sampling, "bias" refers to:
A) The natural variability between samples.
B) The tendency of a sampling method to over- or under-estimate a population parameter.
C) The margin of error in a confidence interval.
D) A small sample size.

Answer: B
Explanation: Sampling bias is a systematic error introduced by a non-random sampling method, causing the sample to not be representative of the population and consistently producing statistics that are different from the parameter.

Q682. (Ch 1) What is z if z * z̅ = 25 and z+z̅ = 8?
A) 5+3i
B) 4+3i
C) 3+4i
D) 8+5i

Answer: B
Explanation: Let z=a+bi. z+z̅=2a=8 => a=4.
z*z̅=a²+b²=25 => 4²+b²=25 => 16+b²=25 => b²=9 => b=±3. So z could be 4+3i or 4-3i. Option B is one of the solutions.

Q683. (Ch 5) Find the equation of the horizontal tangent to the curve y = x³ - 12x.
A) y=16 and y=-16
B) y=0
C) y=2 and y=-2
D) x=16 and x=-16

Answer: A
Explanation: Horizontal tangents occur where the derivative is zero. y' = 3x² - 12. Set to 0: 3x²=12 => x²=4 => x=±2.
We need the equation of the tangent line, which is a horizontal line y=c.
At x=2, y = 2³-12(2)=8-24=-16. So y=-16 is one tangent.
At x=-2, y = (-2)³-12(-2)=-8+24=16. So y=16 is the other tangent.

Q684. (Ch 6) If f(x)dx on [1,5] is the integrand, what are a and b?
A) a=1, b=5 are the limits of integration.
B) a=1, b=5 are the variables.
C) a=1, b=5 are the constants of integration.
D) a=1, b=5 are the functions.

Answer: A
Explanation: In the notation ∫[a,b] f(x) dx, a is the lower limit of integration and b is the upper limit.

Q685. (Ch 8) For non-zero vectors u and v, if u·v=|u||v|, then the vectors are:
A) Orthogonal
B) Pointing in the same direction.
C) Pointing in opposite directions.
D) At a 45° angle.

Answer: B
Explanation: The definition is u·v=|u||v|cosθ. For this to equal |u||v|, cosθ must be 1. This happens when the angle θ is 0°, meaning the vectors are parallel and point in the same direction.

Q686. (Ch 1) A complex number z lies on the unit circle. What can be said about 1/z?
A) 1/z = z
B) 1/z = -z
C) 1/z = z̅
D) |1/z| > 1

Answer: C
Explanation: If z is on the unit circle, |z|=1. We also know that z*z̅ = |z|² = 1. Dividing by z gives z̅ = 1/z.

Q687. (Ch 3) What is the maximum number of times the graph of y = x⁵-x can intersect the line y = 1?
A) 1
B) 3
C) 4
D) 5

Answer: D
Explanation: We are looking for the number of solutions to x⁵-x=1, or x⁵-x-1=0. The number of real roots of a polynomial of degree n is at most n. So there can be at most 5 intersections.

Q688. (Ch 4) What is the range of the function y = e^x?
A) All real numbers
B) All positive real numbers
C) All non-negative real numbers
D) x > 0

Answer: B
Explanation: The graph of y=e^x is always above the x-axis. It can get arbitrarily close to zero but never reaches it. The range is y > 0, or (0, ∞).

Q689. (Ch 5) lim (x→0) x / |x|
A) 1
B) -1
C) 0
D) Does not exist

Answer: D
Explanation: We need to check the one-sided limits.
As x→0⁺, x/|x| = x/x = 1.
As x→0⁻, x/|x| = x/(-x) = -1.
Since the left and right limits are not equal, the two-sided limit does not exist.

Q690. (Ch 12) In linear regression, if all data points lie perfectly on a line with a positive slope, then r = ?
A) 0
B) -1
C) 1
D) 0.5

Answer: C
Explanation: A perfect linear relationship means |r|=1. A positive slope means the correlation is positive, so r=1.

Q691. (Ch 1) Evaluate i⁴k+3 where k is an integer.
A) i
B) -1
C) -i
D) 1

Answer: C
Explanation: i⁴k+3 = (i⁴)k * i³ = (1)k * i³ = 1 * i³ = -i.

Q692. (Ch 2) Use the binomial approximation of (1+x)ⁿ to estimate 1.01¹⁰.
A) 1.1
B) 1.01
C) 1.001
D) 1.2

Answer: A
Explanation: (1+0.01)¹⁰. Using (1+x)ⁿ ≈ 1+nx.
≈ 1 + 10(0.01) = 1 + 0.1 = 1.1.

Q693. (Ch 9) A matrix that is its own inverse is called:
A) Invertible
B) Involutory
C) Singular
D) Symmetric

Answer: B
Explanation: An involutory matrix is a matrix A such that A²=I, which means it is its own inverse (A⁻¹=A). The identity matrix is a simple example.

Q694. (Ch 10) Is the set of all polynomials of degree 2 a vector space?
A) Yes
B) No

Answer: B
Explanation: No. Consider (x²+5x) and (-x²+3x). Both are degree 2. Their sum is 8x, which is degree 1. It is not closed under addition. The set of all polynomials of degree at most 2 is a vector space.

Q695. (Ch 11) The "and" rule in probability corresponds to which set operation?
A) Union (∪)
B) Intersection (∩)
C) Complement (')
D) Difference (-)

Answer: B
Explanation: The probability of A and B occurring is P(A∩B), the probability of the intersection of the two events.

Q696. (Ch 12) Which of these would NOT be a valid null hypothesis?
A) H₀: μ = 50
B) H₀: p ≤ 0.5
C) H₀: x̄ = 50
D) H₀: σ² = 25

Answer: C
Explanation: Hypotheses are statements about unknown population parameters (μ, p, σ), not about sample statistics (x̄, p̂, s) which are known values calculated from the data.

Q697. (Ch 1) If z = cosθ + isinθ, what is 1/z?
A) cosθ - isinθ
B) sinθ + icosθ
C) -cosθ - isinθ
D) 1

Answer: A
Explanation: Since z is on the unit circle, 1/z = z̅. The conjugate of cosθ+isinθ is cosθ-isinθ.

Q698. (Ch 8) Find the cross product <1,0,0> × <0,1,0>.
A) <1,1,0>
B) <0,0,1>
C) <0,0,0>
D) 1

Answer: B
Explanation: This is i × j, which equals k, the vector <0,0,1>.

Q699. (Ch 6) What is the value of ∫[-5,5] sin(x³) dx?
A) 10
B) 5
C) 0
D) 2cos(125)

Answer: C
Explanation: Let f(x)=sin(x³). f(-x)=sin((-x)³)=sin(-x³)=-sin(x³)=-f(x). The function is odd. The integral of an odd function over a symmetric interval [-a,a] is always 0.

Q700. (Ch 5) A function has f'(x) = (x-2)(x-4). Where does f(x) have a local minimum?
A) x=2
B) x=4
C) x=3
D) It has no local minimum.

Answer: B
Explanation: Critical points are at x=2, x=4. Create a sign chart for f'.
If x<2, f' is (+)(-)=+. (Inc)
If 2<x<4, f' is (+)(-)=-. (Dec)
If x>4, f' is (+)(+)=+. (Inc)
At x=2, the function changes from increasing to decreasing (local max).
At x=4, the function changes from decreasing to increasing (local min).

Q701. (Ch 9) Which property is always true for square matrices A and B?
A) AB = BA
B) A+B = B+A
C) (A+B)² = A² + 2AB + B²
D) If AB=AC, then B=C

Answer: B
Explanation: Matrix addition is commutative. A, C, and D are false because matrix multiplication is not commutative. For C, (A+B)²=(A+B)(A+B) = A²+AB+BA+B², which only equals A²+2AB+B² if AB=BA.

Q702. (Ch 12) In a hypothesis test for a mean, if the population standard deviation is unknown, which test statistic should generally be used?
A) z-statistic
B) t-statistic
C) F-statistic
D) χ²-statistic

Answer: B
Explanation: The t-statistic is specifically designed for situations involving inference on a population mean when the population standard deviation σ is unknown and must be estimated by the sample standard deviation s.

Q703. (Ch 1) What is i¹⁰⁰?
A) 1
B) -1
C) i
D) -i

Answer: A
Explanation: 100 is perfectly divisible by 4 (100=4*25). The powers of i repeat every 4 terms, so i¹⁰⁰ is the same as i⁴, which is 1.

Q704. (Ch 8) Find the magnitude of the vector u=i-j+k.
A) 3
B) 1
C) √3
D) 0

Answer: C
Explanation: |u| = √(1²+(-1)²+1²) = √(1+1+1) = √3.

Q705. (Ch 6) The integral ∫ xcos(x) dx is found using integration by parts. The result is:
A) xsin(x) + cos(x) + C
B) xsin(x) - cos(x) + C
C) -xsin(x) + cos(x) + C
D) (x²/2)sin(x) + C

Answer: A
Explanation: Let u=x, dv=cos(x)dx. Then du=dx, v=sin(x).
uv - ∫vdu = xsin(x) - ∫sin(x)dx = xsin(x) - (-cos(x)) + C = xsin(x) + cos(x) + C.

Q706. (Ch 3) If P(x) = (x-1)(x-2), find the y-intercept of the graph y=P(x).
A) 1
B) 2
C) -1
D) -2

Answer: B
Explanation: The y-intercept occurs at x=0. P(0) = (0-1)(0-2) = (-1)(-2) = 2.

Q707. (Ch 4) What is log₅(5³)?
A) 5
B) 3
C) 15
D) 125

Answer: B
Explanation: Using the power rule, log₅(5³) = 3 log₅(5) = 3 * 1 = 3.

Q708. (Ch 5) lim (x→1) (x-1)/(√x - 1) = ?
A) 0
B) 1
C) 2
D) Does not exist

Answer: C
Explanation: Rationalize the denominator by multiplying top and bottom by (√x+1).
[(x-1)(√x+1)]/[(√x-1)(√x+1)] = [(x-1)(√x+1)]/(x-1) = √x+1.
The limit is now lim (x→1) (√x+1) = √1+1=2.

Chapter 1: Complex Numbers (Final Batch)

Q709. The geometric interpretation of adding two complex numbers, z₁ and z₂, corresponds to the:
A) Dot product of their vectors.
B) Tip-to-tail vector addition (or parallelogram rule).
C) Area of the triangle they form.
D) Angle between their vectors.

Answer: B
Explanation: In the Argand diagram, adding z₁ and z₂ is geometrically equivalent to placing the tail of the vector for z₂ at the tip of the vector for z₁, with the resultant vector being the sum.

Q710. If z is a non-zero complex number, arg(z) + arg(z̅) = ? (Assuming principal arguments not summing outside (-π, π] range)
A) 0
B) π
C) π/2
D) 2π

Answer: A
Explanation: arg(z) = φ and arg(z̅) = -φ. Their sum is φ + (-φ) = 0.

Q711. Find the value of 1/z if z = 2(cos(π/3) + i sin(π/3)).
A) 0.5(cos(π/3) - i sin(π/3))
B) 2(cos(π/3) - i sin(π/3))
C) 0.5(cos(π/3) + i sin(π/3))
D) -2(cos(π/3) + i sin(π/3))

Answer: A
Explanation: 1/z = z⁻¹. By De Moivre's theorem, rⁿcis(nφ).
Here, r=2, n=-1, φ=π/3.
So 1/z = 2⁻¹cis(-π/3) = 0.5(cos(-π/3)+isin(-π/3)) = 0.5(cos(π/3)-isin(π/3)).

Q712. What are the solutions to z² = -1?
A) 1, -1
B) i, -i
C) i, 1
D) -i, -1

Answer: B
Explanation: By definition, i = √-1, so i² = -1. Also, (-i)² = (-1)²i² = 1*(-1) = -1.

Chapter 2: Binomial Theorem and Series Expansions (Final Batch)
