Find constant term in (2x - 1/x)⁶. Gen term: ⁶Cᵣ(2x)⁶⁻ʳ(-1/x)ʳ=⁶Cᵣ 2⁶⁻ʳ (-1)ʳ x⁶⁻²ʳ. r=3. Term: ⁶C₃ 2³ (-1)³=208(-1)=-160.
Find constant term in (x/2 - 2/x)⁶. Gen term: ⁶Cᵣ(x/2)⁶⁻ʳ(-2/x)ʳ = ⁶Cᵣ (1/2)⁶⁻ʳ (-2)ʳ x⁶⁻²ʳ. r=3. Term: ⁶C₃(1/2)³(-2)³ = 20*(1/8)*(-8) = -20.
Let's try to get 672. What's 672 / 84? 8. So I need (-2)³ from the coefficient. This means r must be 3. The binomial coefficient must be 84. What C r = 84? ⁹C₃ = 84.
Let's try (2x - 1/x)⁹. Term x⁹⁻²ʳ. 9-2r=0 doesn't have integer r.
The initial calculation 84 * 64 was likely the source of confusion. Let me correct the options to be right for the original question.
(x² - 2/x)⁹. r=6. Term is ⁹C₆(-2)⁶ = 84 * 64 = 5376.
Q713. (Revised with correct options) Find the constant term in the expansion of (x² - 2/x)⁹.
A) -5376
B) 5376
C) 84
D) -84

Answer: B
Explanation: As calculated, r=6. The term is ⁹C₆(-2)⁶ = 84 * (+64) = 5376.

Q714. If (1+2x)ⁿ = 1 + 8x + 24x² + ..., find n.
A) 4
B) 8
C) 3
D) 2

Answer: A
Explanation: Term in x is n(2x)=2nx. Term in x² is n(n-1)/2² = [n(n-1)/2]4x² = 2n(n-1)x².
From x term: 2n=8 => n=4.
Check with x² term: 2(4)(4-1)x² = 8(3)x²=24x². It matches. So n=4.

Q715. Which is the best approximation for √0.98 using the first two terms of a binomial expansion?
A) 0.99
B) 0.98
C) 1.01
D) 0.9

Answer: A
Explanation: √0.98 = (1 - 0.02)¹/². Using (1-x)ⁿ ≈ 1-nx.
≈ 1 - (1/2)(0.02) = 1 - 0.01 = 0.99.

Chapter 3: Polynomials and Rational Functions (Final Batch)

Q716. A cubic polynomial with real coefficients MUST have:
A) At least one real root.
B) At least one complex root.
C) Three real roots.
D) Three complex roots.

Answer: A
Explanation: Complex roots for real-coefficient polynomials must come in conjugate pairs. Since the degree is odd (3), there must be at least one root left over that cannot be paired. This must be a real root. Geometrically, the ends of a cubic go in opposite directions, so the graph must cross the x-axis at least once.

Q717. The function f(x) = (x³+x)/(x²+1) simplifies to:
A) y = x
B) y = x + 1/x
C) y = 1
D) It cannot be simplified.

Answer: A
Explanation: Factor the numerator: x(x²+1). The function is x(x²+1)/(x²+1) = x. Since the denominator x²+1 is never zero, there are no holes or asymptotes. The function is identical to y=x.

Q718. How many non-real roots does the polynomial P(x)=(x²+4)(x²-5)(x+1) have?
A) 0
B) 2
C) 3
D) 4

Answer: B
Explanation: (x²+4)=0 gives x=±2i (2 non-real roots).
(x²-5)=0 gives x=±√5 (2 real roots).
(x+1)=0 gives x=-1 (1 real root).
Total non-real roots = 2.

Chapter 4: Logarithmic and Exponential Functions (Final Batch)

Q719. What is the value of log₃(27√3)?
A) 3
B) 3.5
C) 4
D) 2.5

Answer: B
Explanation: 27√3 = 3³ * 3¹/² = 3^(3.5). So, log₃(3^(3.5)) = 3.5.

Q720. If log_a(b) = c, then log_(1/a)(b) = ?
A) c
B) 1/c
C) -c
D) -1/c

Answer: C
Explanation: Let log_(1/a)(b) = x. Then (1/a)^x = b => a⁻ˣ = b.
We are given a^c=b. So a⁻ˣ = a^c => -x=c => x=-c.

Q721. Solve the inequality 2^x > 8.
A) x > 3
B) x < 3
C) x > 4
D) x = 3

Answer: A
Explanation: 2^x > 2³. Since the base (2) is greater than 1, the exponential function is increasing, so we can compare the exponents directly: x > 3.

Chapter 5: Limits, Continuity, and Differentiation (Final Batch)

Q722. L'Hôpital's Rule can be applied to a limit of the form f(x)/g(x) if the limit results in the indeterminate form:
A) 0/0 or ∞/∞
B) 0 * ∞
C) 1^∞
D) ∞ - ∞

Answer: A
Explanation: L'Hôpital's Rule applies directly only to the forms 0/0 and ∞/∞. Other indeterminate forms like B, C, and D must first be algebraically manipulated into one of those two forms.

Q723. The function f(x) has a derivative f'(x)=x². Which statement is true about f(x)?
A) f(x) has a local minimum at x=0.
B) f(x) has a local maximum at x=0.
C) f(x) has a point of inflection at x=0.
D) f(x) has a corner at x=0.

Answer: C
Explanation: f'(x)=x² has a critical point at x=0. However, the sign of f'(x) is positive for x<0 and positive for x>0. Since the derivative does not change sign, there is no max or min. This is the condition for a point of inflection (specifically, where the slope is zero and doesn't change from increasing/decreasing). f''(x)=2x, which changes sign at x=0, confirming it's an inflection point.

Q724. Find dy/dx if y = 2^x.
A) 2^x
B) x * 2^(x-1)
C) 2^x ln(2)
D) 2^x / ln(2)

Answer: C
Explanation: This is a standard derivative rule: d/dx(a^x) = a^x ln(a).

Q725. If velocity v(t) = 3t²+1, then the displacement of the particle from t=0 to t=2 is:
A) 10
B) 9
C) 13
D) 12

Answer: A
Explanation: Displacement is the definite integral of velocity.
∫[0,2] (3t²+1)dt = [t³+t]_[0,2] = (2³+2) - (0³+0) = 8+2=10.

Chapter 6: Integration (Final Batch)

Q726. Evaluate ∫[-1,1] 1 / (1+x²) dx.
A) π/2
B) π
C) π/4
D) 0

Answer: A
Explanation: ∫ 1/(1+x²)dx = tan⁻¹(x). Evaluating: [tan⁻¹(x)]_[-1,1] = tan⁻¹(1) - tan⁻¹(-1) = (π/4) - (-π/4) = π/2.

Q727. If ∫[0,x] f(t)dt = x sin(x), what is f(x)?
A) sin(x) + xcos(x)
B) x sin(x)
C) cos(x)
D) x cos(x)

Answer: A
Explanation: By FTC Part 1, f(x) is the derivative of the integral expression. So f(x) = d/dx(x sin(x)). Using the product rule: (1)sin(x) + x(cos(x)) = sin(x) + xcos(x).

Q728. To integrate a function involving √(a²-x²), a common first step is to use the trigonometric substitution:
A) x = a tanθ
B) x = a secθ
C) x = a sinθ
D) x = a coshθ

Answer: C
Explanation: If x=asinθ, then the term becomes √(a² - a²sin²θ) = √(a²(1-sin²θ)) = √(a²cos²θ) = acosθ, which eliminates the square root.

Chapter 7: Ordinary Differential Equations (Final Batch)

Q729. Find a general solution to y'' = 4y.
A) y = C₁e^(4x) + C₂e^(-4x)
B) y = C₁cos(4x) + C₂sin(4x)
C) y = C₁e^(2x) + C₂e^(-2x)
D) y = C₁cos(2x) + C₂sin(2x)

Answer: C
Explanation: The DE is y''-4y=0. The characteristic equation is m²-4=0 => m²=4 => m=±2. Distinct real roots lead to the solution y = C₁e^(2x) + C₂e^(-2x).

Q730. If an object is heated, according to Newton's Law of Cooling/Heating, the constant k in dT/dt = -k(T-T_a) is:
A) still positive
B) negative
C) zero
D) dependent on temperature

Answer: A
Explanation: The constant k (thermal conductivity constant) is always considered positive. The physics is handled by the term (T-T_a). If the object is cooler (T<T_a), then T-T_a is negative, and dT/dt = -k(negative) = positive, meaning the temperature increases, which is correct for heating.

Chapter 8: Vectors in Two and Three Dimensions (Final Batch)

Q731. The curl of a vector field (∇ × F) measures its:
A) Source strength
B) Rate of change
C) Local rotation or circulation
D) Gradient

Answer: C
Explanation: The curl operation measures the microscopic rotational effect of a vector field. If ∇×F = 0, the field is irrotational.

Q732. Three points P, Q, R are collinear if:
A) The vector PQ is perpendicular to PR.
B) The vector PQ is parallel to PR.
C) The points form a right-angled triangle.
D) The distance PQ equals the distance QR.

Answer: B
Explanation: If three points lie on the same line, then the vector from one to the second must be parallel to the vector from one to the third (i.e., one is a scalar multiple of the other).

Q733. If |u| = 3, |v| = 4, and the angle between them is 60°, find |u-v|.
A) √13
B) 5
C) √37
D) 1

Answer: A
Explanation: Use the Law of Cosines on the triangle formed by u, v, and u-v. The angle inside this triangle, opposite the side u-v, is 60°.
|u-v|² = |u|² + |v|² - 2|u||v|cos(60°) = 3²+4² - 2(3)(4)(1/2) = 9+16-12 = 13.
So |u-v| = √13.

Chapter 9: Matrices and Determinants (Final Batch)

Q734. The trace of a square matrix is the:
A) Determinant of the matrix.
B) Product of the diagonal elements.
C) Sum of the diagonal elements.
D) Transpose of the matrix.

Answer: C
Explanation: The trace, Tr(A), is defined as the sum of the elements on the main diagonal.

Q735. Let A=[[1,2],[0,1]] and B=[[1,0],[3,1]]. Find (A-B).
A) [[0,2],[-3,0]]
B) [[0,2],[3,0]]
C) [[2,2],[3,2]]
D) [[-1,-2],[3,-2]]

Answer: A
Explanation: Subtract corresponding elements: [[1-1, 2-0], [0-3, 1-1]] = [[0,2],[-3,0]].

Q736. For any square matrix A, A - A^T is always:
A) Symmetric
B) Skew-symmetric
C) The zero matrix
D) The identity matrix

Answer: B
Explanation: Let B = A - A^T. Then B^T = (A-A^T)^T = A^T - (A^T)^T = A^T - A = -(A - A^T) = -B. Since B^T = -B, it is skew-symmetric.

Chapter 10: Introduction to Vector Spaces (Final Batch)

Q737. Which of the following is a scalar, given A is an n x n matrix?
A) The null space of A.
B) An eigenvector of A.
C) A linear combination of columns of A.
D) An eigenvalue of A.

Answer: D
Explanation: An eigenvalue (λ) is the scalar factor by which an eigenvector is stretched or shrunk by a linear transformation. All the other options are vectors or sets of vectors (spaces).

Q738. In a vector space, a vector's representation is unique with respect to:
A) The origin
B) The space itself
C) A given basis
D) Any spanning set

Answer: C
Explanation: Once a basis is chosen for a vector space, every vector in that space has a unique representation as a linear combination of those basis vectors. The coefficients of this combination are the vector's unique coordinates relative to that basis.

Q739. If A is an invertible matrix, its eigenvalues are all:
A) Zero
B) Non-zero
C) Positive
D) Real

Answer: B
Explanation: A matrix is invertible if and only if its determinant is non-zero. The determinant is the product of the eigenvalues. For the product to be non-zero, all the individual eigenvalues must be non-zero.

Chapter 11: Advanced Probability and Statistics (Final Batch)

Q740. Which statement about probability is ALWAYS true for any event A?
A) 0 ≤ P(A) ≤ 1
B) P(A) + P(A') = 0
C) P(A) > 0
D) P(A) < 1

Answer: A
Explanation: By the axioms of probability, the probability of any event must be a number between 0 (impossible) and 1 (certain), inclusive.

Q741. In a class of 30 students, 20 play football and 15 play basketball. 10 play both. How many play neither?
A) 0
B) 5
C) 10
D) 15

Answer: B
Explanation: Use the principle of inclusion-exclusion. Total playing at least one = P(F) + P(B) - P(F∩B) = 20 + 15 - 10 = 25.
Total students = 30. Those who play neither = Total - (those playing at least one) = 30 - 25 = 5.

Q742. A Z-score of -1.5 means the data point is:
A) 1.5 standard deviations above the mean.
B) 1.5 times the mean.
C) 1.5 standard deviations below the mean.
D) At the mean.

Answer: C
Explanation: The Z-score measures how many standard deviations a point is from the mean. A negative sign indicates it is below the mean.

Chapter 12: Statistical Inference (Final Batch)

Q743. What is the margin of error in the confidence interval (52.5, 57.5)?
A) 5
B) 2.5
C) 55
D) 5.2

Answer: B
Explanation: The width of the interval is 57.5 - 52.5 = 5. The margin of error is half the width. ME = 5 / 2 = 2.5. The point estimate is 55 ± 2.5.

Q744. In a goodness-of-fit test, the expected frequency for a category is calculated by:
A) Counting the observations in that category.
B) n * p, where p is the hypothesized proportion for that category.
C) Dividing the observed frequency by n.
D) The test statistic.

Answer: B
Explanation: To test if the observed data fits a hypothesized distribution, you calculate the expected counts for each category by multiplying the total sample size (n) by the hypothesized probability (p) for that category.

Q745. The line of best fit in linear regression is the one that minimizes:
A) The sum of the squared residuals.
B) The correlation coefficient.
C) The sum of the residuals.
D) The number of outliers.

Answer: A
Explanation: This is the definition of the least-squares regression line. It is mathematically optimized to minimize the sum of the squared vertical distances (residuals) from the data points to the line.

Q746. Which of the following correlation coefficients indicates the weakest linear relationship?
A) r = 0.5
B) r = -0.7
C) r = 0.9
D) r = -0.1

Answer: D
Explanation: The strength of the linear relationship is determined by how close |r| is to 1. The value closest to 0 indicates the weakest linear relationship. |-0.1|=0.1, which is the smallest absolute value among the options.

Final Mixed Review Questions

Q747. (Ch 1) Simplify e^(iπ) + e^(-iπ).
A) 0
B) -2
C) 2
D) 2i

Answer: B
Explanation: e^(iπ) = -1. e^(-iπ) = cos(-π)+isin(-π) = -1. The sum is -1 + (-1) = -2. Alternatively, this is 2cos(π) = 2(-1) = -2.

Q748. (Ch 5) lim (x→-3) (x²+x-6)/(x+3) = ?
A) -5
B) 5
C) 1
D) Does not exist

Answer: A
Explanation: It's 0/0 form. Factor the numerator: (x+3)(x-2)/(x+3) = x-2.
The limit is lim(x→-3) (x-2) = -3-2 = -5.

Q749. (Ch 9) A matrix A satisfies A=A⁻¹. Find det(A).
A) 1 only
B) -1 only
C) 1 or -1
D) 0

Answer: C
Explanation: If A=A⁻¹, then A²=I. So det(A²)=det(I) => (det A)² = 1 => det A = ±1.

Q750. (Ch 11) For any two events A and B, P(A∪B) = ?
A) P(A)+P(B)
B) P(A)+P(B)-P(A∩B)
C) P(A)P(B)
D) P(A|B)P(B)

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the general addition rule of probability. The simpler P(A)+P(B) only applies if the events are mutually exclusive.

Q751. (Ch 8) Find the angle between the line r=t<1,1,1> and the plane x+y+z=5.
A) 0°
B) 30°
C) 45°
D) 90°

Answer: D
Explanation: The line's direction vector v=<1,1,1> is the same as the plane's normal vector n=<1,1,1>. This means the line is perpendicular (normal) to the plane. The angle between them is 90°.

Q752. (Ch 6) Evaluate ∫ x √(x²+1) dx.
A) (1/3)(x²+1)^(3/2) + C
B) (2/3)(x²+1)^(3/2) + C
C) (x²/2) * (2/3)(x²+1)^(3/2) + C
D) (1/2)(x²+1)^(1/2) + C

Answer: A
Explanation: Use u-sub. u=x²+1, du=2xdx => xdx=du/2.
∫√u (du/2) = (1/2)∫u¹/²du = (1/2) [u³/²/(3/2)]+C = (1/2)(2/3)u³/²+C = (1/3)(x²+1)³/²+C.

Q753. (Ch 3) If P(x) = (x²-4)(x-2), at x=2 the graph has a:
A) simple root
B) double root
C) triple root
D) x-intercept but no root.

Answer: B
Explanation: P(x)=(x-2)(x+2)(x-2) = (x-2)²(x+2). The factor (x-2) is squared, indicating a double root (multiplicity 2) at x=2.

Q754. (Ch 7) If a characteristic equation has roots m=±5i, the system represents:
A) Overdamped motion
B) Critically damped motion
C) Undamped oscillation (SHM)
D) Exponential growth

Answer: C
Explanation: Purely imaginary roots (α=0) signify an undamped system that will oscillate forever. The solution is y=C₁cos(5t)+C₂sin(5t).

Q755. (Ch 2) Find the coefficient of the x³ term in the Maclaurin series for f(x)=cos(2x).
A) 0
B) -8/6
C) 8/6
D) 1/6

Answer: A
Explanation: The function f(x)=cos(2x) is an even function. Its Maclaurin series will contain only even powers of x. Therefore, the coefficient of any odd power like x³ must be zero.

Q756. (Ch 1) What is |3-4i| / |6-8i|?
A) 2
B) 1/2
C) 1
D) 1/4

Answer: B
Explanation: |3-4i|=5. |6-8i| = √(36+64)=√100=10. The ratio is 5/10 = 1/2. Alternatively, 6-8i = 2(3-4i), so |6-8i|=|2||3-4i|=2|3-4i|. The ratio is 1/2.

Q757. (Ch 5) Find lim (x→4) (x-4) / (x²-16).
A) 1/8
B) 8
C) 1/4
D) 0

Answer: A
Explanation: Factor denominator: (x-4)/[(x-4)(x+4)] = 1/(x+4). The limit is lim(x→4) 1/(x+4) = 1/(4+4) = 1/8.

Q758. (Ch 9) For matrices, (A^T)^T = ?
A) A
B) A⁻¹
C) A²
D) I

Answer: A
Explanation: Transposing a matrix twice returns the original matrix.

Q759. (Ch 11) A test has a 99% probability of giving a correct result. What is the probability it gives an incorrect result?
A) 0.99
B) 0.01
C) 0.1
D) 1.01

Answer: B
Explanation: The event "incorrect result" is the complement of "correct result". P(Incorrect) = 1 - P(Correct) = 1 - 0.99 = 0.01.

Q760. (Ch 12) In the equation for a confidence interval, Point Estimate ± Margin of Error, the Margin of Error is:
A) (Critical Value) / (Standard Error)
B) (Standard Error) / (Critical Value)
C) (Point Estimate) * (Standard Error)
D) (Critical Value) * (Standard Error)

Answer: D
Explanation: The margin of error is calculated by multiplying the critical value (Z* or t*, which depends on the confidence level) by the standard error of the statistic (e.g., s/√n or √(p̂q̂/n)).

... Continuing to the end ...

Q761. (Ch 1) In the Argand plane, multiplying by -1 is equivalent to a rotation of:
A) 90°
B) 180°
C) 270°
D) 360°

Answer: B
Explanation: -1 as a complex number is 1(cosπ + isinπ). Multiplying by it adds π (180°) to the argument of any complex number, which is a rotation of 180°.

Q762. (Ch 2) The value of ¹⁰C₀ + ¹⁰C₁ + ... + ¹⁰C₁₀ is:
A) 10!
B) 100
C) 2¹⁰
D) 10²

Answer: C
Explanation: This is the sum of all coefficients in the expansion of (1+1)¹⁰ = 2¹⁰.

Q763. (Ch 3) If x=2 is a root of x³-6x²+kx-6=0, find k.
A) 11
B) -11
C) 5
D) -5

Answer: A
Explanation: P(2)=0 => 2³-6(2²)+k(2)-6=0 => 8-24+2k-6=0 => 2k-22=0 => 2k=22 => k=11.

Q764. (Ch 4) Solve 9^x = 27.
A) 1.5
B) 2
C) 3
D) 0.5

Answer: A
Explanation: Write both with base 3: (3²)ˣ = 3³ => 3^(2x) = 3³. 2x=3 => x=1.5.

Q765. (Ch 5) Find the equation of the tangent to y = e^x at x=0.
A) y = x
B) y = x + 1
C) y = 1
D) y = e

Answer: B
Explanation: y'=e^x. At x=0, slope m=e⁰=1. The point is (0, e⁰)=(0,1). Eq: y-1=1(x-0) => y=x+1.

Q766. (Ch 6) Evaluate ∫ dx/x² from 1 to 2.
A) -1/2
B) 1/2
C) 3/4
D) -3/8

Answer: B
Explanation: ∫x⁻²dx = -x⁻¹ = -1/x. Eval: [-1/x]_[1,2] = (-1/2) - (-1/1) = -1/2+1 = 1/2.

Q767. (Ch 7) What is the standard form of the linear DE: x(dy/dx) = 5 - 2y?
A) y' + (2/x)y = 5/x
B) y' + 2y = 5
C) y' - 2y = 5/x
D) y' + (5/x) = 2y/x

Answer: A
Explanation: Standard form is y'+P(x)y=Q(x). x y' + 2y = 5. Divide by x: y' + (2/x)y = 5/x.

Q768. (Ch 8) Find the dot product of two vectors if their magnitudes are 5 and 6, and the angle between them is 60°.
A) 15
B) 30
C) 15√3
D) 11

Answer: A
Explanation: u·v = |u||v|cosθ = 5 * 6 * cos(60°) = 30 * (1/2) = 15.

Q769. (Ch 9) A matrix A is idempotent if:
A) A = A^T
B) A = A⁻¹
C) A² = A
D) A^k = 0 for some k.

Answer: C
Explanation: This is the definition of an idempotent matrix. Applying the transformation multiple times is the same as applying it once.

Q770. (Ch 10) In R³, any set of 4 vectors is:
A) a basis
B) a spanning set
C) linearly independent
D) linearly dependent

Answer: D
Explanation: The dimension of R³ is 3. Any set containing more than 3 vectors must be linearly dependent.

Q771. (Ch 11) A spinner has 4 equal sectors: Red, Blue, Green, Yellow. P(Red or Green) = ?
A) 1/4
B) 1
C) 1/2
D) 1/16

Answer: C
Explanation: The events are mutually exclusive. P(R or G) = P(R)+P(G) = 1/4 + 1/4 = 2/4 = 1/2.

Q772. (Ch 12) Degrees of freedom for a chi-square test for independence with a 3x4 contingency table is:
A) 12
B) 7
C) 6
D) 5

Answer: C
Explanation: df = (rows-1)(columns-1) = (3-1)(4-1) = 2*3 = 6.

Q773. (Ch 1) Find the reciprocal of 2i.
A) -2i
B) 2
C) -0.5i
D) 0.5i

Answer: C
Explanation: 1/(2i) = i/(2i²) = i/(2*(-1)) = -i/2 = -0.5i.

Q774. (Ch 2) Using Maclaurin series, sin(x) ≈ x for small x. This is a ____ order approximation.
A) zero
B) first
C) second
D) third

Answer: B
Explanation: Since the highest power of x used is 1, this is a first-order approximation.

Q775. (Ch 3) If (x+2) is a factor of P(x), then which must be true?
A) P(2)=0
B) P(-2)=0
C) P(0)=2
D) P(0)=-2

Answer: B
Explanation: The Factor Theorem states that (x-c) is a factor if and only if P(c)=0. Here, c=-2.

Q776. (Ch 4) Express log_b(x) in terms of log_x.
A) 1 / log_x(b)
B) log_b(x)
C) log_x(1/b)
D) -log_x(b)

Answer: A
Explanation: Use change of base to base x: log_b(x) = log_x(x)/log_x(b) = 1/log_x(b).

Q777. (Ch 5) If f''(x) > 0 on an interval, the graph of f(x) is:
A) Concave up
B) Concave down
C) Increasing
D) Decreasing

Answer: A
Explanation: A positive second derivative means the slope (the first derivative) is increasing. This corresponds to the curve being shaped like a cup (concave up).

Q778. (Ch 6) If f(x) and g(x) are antiderivatives of the same function h(x), then f(x)-g(x) is:
A) 0
B) h'(x)
C) a constant
D) h(x)

Answer: C
Explanation: Any two antiderivatives of the same function can only differ by a constant.

Q779. (Ch 7) An ODE contains derivatives of a function of only a single independent variable.
A) True
B) False

Answer: A
Explanation: This is the definition of an Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE). If there were derivatives with respect to multiple independent variables, it would be a PDE.

Q780. (Ch 8) Find the parametric equations for the plane containing point (1,1,1) and parallel to vectors <1,0,0> and <0,1,0>.
A) x=1+t, y=1, z=1
B) x=1+t, y=1+s, z=1
C) x=1, y=t, z=s
D) x=1+t+s, y=1+t+s, z=1+t+s

Answer: B
Explanation: The parametric form is r = r₀ + tu + sv.
r = <1,1,1> + t<1,0,0> + s<0,1,0>.
x=1+t, y=1+s, z=1. This is the xy-plane shifted up to z=1.

Q7d1. (Ch 9) For any invertible matrix A, (A⁻¹)^T = ?
A) (A^T)⁻¹
B) A
C) A^T
D) A⁻¹

Answer: A
Explanation: Taking the transpose and taking the inverse are interchangeable operations.

Q782. (Ch 10) The dimension of a vector space is the:
A) Number of vectors in the space.
B) Largest number of linearly independent vectors.
C) Number of vectors in any basis for the space.
D) Number of subspaces.

Answer: C
Explanation: This is the definition of dimension. All bases for a given space have the same number of vectors.

Q783. (Ch 11) When is P(A and B) = P(A) * P(B)?
A) Always
B) If A and B are mutually exclusive.
C) If A and B are independent.
D) Never

Answer: C
Explanation: This multiplication rule only holds if the events A and B are independent.

Q784. (Ch 12) To test a claim about a population variance, one would use a:
A) Z-test
B) T-test
C) F-test
D) Chi-square (χ²) test

Answer: D
Explanation: The chi-square distribution is used for inference (confidence intervals and hypothesis tests) about a single population variance or standard deviation.

Q785. (Ch 1) Simplify (2-3i)-(5-3i).
A) -3
B) -3-6i
C) 7-6i
D) 7

Answer: A
Explanation: (2-5) + (-3 - (-3))i = -3 + 0i = -3.

Q786. (Ch 2) The binomial coefficients nCr can be found in:
A) The periodic table
B) A logarithmic table
C) A Z-table
D) Pascal's triangle

Answer: D
Explanation: The rows of Pascal's triangle are constructed from the binomial coefficients. Row n contains the values nC0, nC1, ..., nCn.

Q787. (Ch 3) If a cubic polynomial has real roots 1, 2, 3, its equation could be:
A) x³ - 6x² + 11x - 6 = 0
B) x³ + 6x² + 11x + 6 = 0
C) x³ - 6 = 0
D) x³ - 6x = 0

Answer: A
Explanation: (x-1)(x-2)(x-3) = (x²-3x+2)(x-3) = x³-3x²-3x²+9x+2x-6 = x³-6x²+11x-6=0.

Q788. (Ch 4) What is log₅(10) + log₅(12.5)?
A) log₅(22.5)
B) 25
C) 2
D) 3

Answer: D
Explanation: log₅(10*12.5) = log₅(125) = log₅(5³) = 3.

Q789. (Ch 5) Find lim (x→-2) 5.
A) 5
B) -2
C) -10
D) 0

Answer: A
Explanation: The limit of a constant is just the constant itself, regardless of what x approaches.

Q790. (Ch 6) Evaluate ∫ dx / √x.
A) 2√x + C
B) (1/2)√x + C
C) -1/(2x√x) + C
D) ln(√x) + C

Answer: A
Explanation: ∫ x⁻¹/² dx = x¹/² / (1/2) + C = 2x¹/² + C = 2√x + C.

Q791. (Ch 7) If a characteristic equation is m²=0, the solution to the DE is:
A) y = C₁ + C₂e^x
B) y = C₁
C) y = C₁ + C₂x
D) y = (C₁+C₂x)e^x

Answer: C
Explanation: m=0 is a repeated real root. The general form is y=(C₁+C₂x)e^(mx). Here, y=(C₁+C₂x)e^(0x) = C₁+C₂x, which is the equation of a line.

Q792. (Ch 8) Find the area of a parallelogram with sides u and v, if u·v = 10, |u|=5, |v|=4.
A) 20
B) 10√3
C) 10
D) Cannot be determined

Answer: B
Explanation: Area = |u||v|sinθ. We need sinθ. We know cosθ = (u·v)/(|u||v|) = 10/(54)=1/2. So θ=60°. sin(60°)=√3/2. Area = 54*(√3/2) = 10√3.

Q793. (Ch 9) For A=[[a,b],[c,d]], if A=-A^T, which MUST be true?
A) a=d=0
B) b=-c
C) Both A and B
D) None of the above.

Answer: C
Explanation: For A to be skew-symmetric, A=-A^T. [[a,b],[c,d]] = -[[a,c],[b,d]] = [[-a,-c],[-b,-d]]. Equating elements: a=-a=>2a=0=>a=0. d=-d=>2d=0=>d=0. b=-c. So both A and B are true.

Q794. (Ch 10) Any two vectors in R³ are...
A) always linearly independent.
B) always linearly dependent.
C) sometimes linearly independent, sometimes dependent.
D) always a basis.

Answer: C
Explanation: If they are parallel, they are dependent. If they are not parallel, they are independent. They cannot be a basis, as 3 vectors are required for R³.

Q795. (Ch 11) A continuous random variable is defined by its:
A) Probability mass function (PMF)
B) Frequency table
C) Probability density function (PDF)
D) Mode

Answer: C
Explanation: Discrete random variables are described by PMFs, while continuous random variables are described by PDFs, under which the area represents probability.

Q796. (Ch 12) In testing a claim that the mean weight of a product is less than 50g (μ < 50g), you would use a:
A) Two-tailed test
B) Right-tailed test
C) Left-tailed test
D) Test for variance.

Answer: C
Explanation: The inequality in the alternative hypothesis determines the tail. The "<" symbol indicates that the region of interest (the "rejection region") is in the lower, or left, tail of the distribution.

Q797. (Ch 1) What is i * z̅ if z=a+bi?
A) -b+ai
B) b-ai
C) b+ai
D) a-bi

Answer: C
Explanation: z̅=a-bi. i*z̅ = i(a-bi) = ai - bi² = ai - b(-1) = b+ai.

Q798. (Ch 2) If the second term of (1+x)ⁿ is -5x, what is n?
A) 5
B) -5
C) 1/5
D) -1/5

Answer: B
Explanation: The second term is nC₁ x¹ = nx. We are given nx = -5x, so n=-5.

Q799. (Ch 5) lim (x→0⁺) 1/x = ?
A) ∞
B) -∞
C) 0
D) Does not exist

Answer: A
Explanation: As x approaches 0 from the positive side, 1/x is the ratio of a positive constant and a very small positive number, which tends towards positive infinity.

Q800. (Ch 9) If A is an invertible matrix, then (A^T)⁻¹ = ?
A) (A⁻¹)^T
B) A
C) A^T
D) A⁻¹

Answer: A
Explanation: Taking the inverse and taking the transpose are commutative operations. This is a standard property.

Q801. (Ch 8) Find a scalar t such that u=<1,t,-2> is orthogonal to v=<4,2,3>.
A) 1
B) -1
C) 2
D) 4

Answer: A
Explanation: Orthogonal means their dot product is 0. u·v = 14+t2+(-2)*3 = 4+2t-6 = 2t-2. Set to 0: 2t-2=0 => t=1.

Q802. (Ch 11) Using a standard deck of 52 cards, what is P(Ace or King)?
A) 8/52
B) 2/52
C) 1/52
D) 16/52

Answer: A
Explanation: The events are mutually exclusive. There are 4 aces and 4 kings. P(A or K) = P(A) + P(K) = 4/52 + 4/52 = 8/52.

Q803. (Ch 6) If f(x) = ∫[0,x] sin(t²)dt, find f'(x).
A) sin(x²)
B) 2xsin(x²)
C) cos(x²)
D) -cos(x²)

Answer: A
Explanation: This is a direct application of FTC Part 1: d/dx[∫[a,x] f(t)dt] = f(x). Here, f(t)=sin(t²), so the derivative is sin(x²).

Q804. (Ch 7) Solve y' = e^y.
A) y = Ce^x
B) y = e^x + C
C) -e^(-y) = x + C
D) y = ln|x| + C

Answer: C
Explanation: Separate: dy/e^y = dx => e⁻ʸ dy = dx. Integrate: -e⁻ʸ = x + C.

Q805. (Ch 12) What happens to the risk of a Type I error if the significance level α is decreased?
A) It increases.
B) It decreases.
C) It stays the same.
D) It depends on the p-value.

Answer: B
Explanation: The significance level α is the probability of making a Type I error. By definition, decreasing α decreases this risk.

Q806. (Ch 3) What is the oblique asymptote of y=(2x³+...)/(x²+...)?
A) y=2
B) y=2x
C) y=2x²
D) No oblique asymptote

Answer: B
Explanation: When the numerator degree is exactly one more than the denominator degree, there is an oblique asymptote. Its equation is the quotient from long division. The leading term of the quotient will be 2x³/x² = 2x. So the asymptote is y=2x+b.

Q807. (Ch 4) What is the y-intercept of the function y = log₂(x+4)?
A) 4
B) 2
C) 0
D) 1

Answer: B
Explanation: Set x=0. y = log₂(0+4) = log₂(4) = 2. The point is (0,2).

Q808. (Ch 10) The number of vectors in a basis is equal to the ____ of the vector space.
A) span
B) dimension
C) null space
D) rank

Answer: B
Explanation: This is the definition of dimension. It is the size of any basis for the space.
